    88	/** Event-shaped: an object carrying a string `type`. */
    89	const isEventShaped = (/** @type {any} */ v) => v !== null && typeof v === 'object' && !Array.isArray(v) && typeof v.type === 'string';
    90	
    91	/**
    92	 * Tolerant stdin parse (live /baton:handoff failure: a model sent one event
    93	 * per line and the whole narrative checkpoint silently failed). Accept the
    94	 * canonical single JSON value, a bare array of events, or NDJSON where every
    95	 * line is an event object or a {schema, events} wrapper. All-or-nothing: one
    96	 * non-event element fails the whole parse — never a partial apply.
    97	 * @param {string} text @returns {{ok: true, raw: any} | {ok: false, why?: string}}
    98	 */
    99	function parseStdin(text) {
   100	  let single;
   101	  let singleOk = false;
   102	  try {
   103	    single = JSON.parse(text);
   104	    singleOk = true;
   105	  } catch {
   106	    singleOk = false;
   107	  }
   108	  if (singleOk) {
   109	    if (Array.isArray(single)) {
   110	      return single.every(isEventShaped) ? { ok: true, raw: { schema: 'baton/event@1', events: single } } : { ok: false };
   111	    }
   112	    // A closest-miss of the documented wrapper — an events array whose schema
   113	    // key is missing or wrong — must ERROR, never silently degrade to a junk
   114	    // note with ok:true (the model cannot self-correct from a success
   115	    // envelope while its narrative was discarded).
   116	    if (single !== null && typeof single === 'object' && Array.isArray(single.events) && single.schema !== 'baton/event@1') {
   117	      return { ok: false, why: `stdin carries an events array but schema is ${single.schema === undefined ? 'missing' : JSON.stringify(single.schema)} — use {"schema":"baton/event@1","events":[…]}` };
   118	    }
   119	    // A single bare event object (string `type`, none of the raw-hook payload
   120	    // markers) is one of the two most likely readings of "pipe events on
   121	    // stdin" — accept it as that event instead of degrading it to a junk note.
   122	    if (
   123	      isEventShaped(single) &&
   124	      single.schema === undefined &&
   125	      !('hook_event_name' in single) &&
   126	      !('session_id' in single) &&
   127	      !('event' in single)
   128	    ) {
   129	      return { ok: true, raw: { schema: 'baton/event@1', events: [single] } };
   130	    }
   131	    return { ok: true, raw: single };
   132	  }
   133	  const lines = text.split('\n').map((l) => l.trim()).filter((l) => l !== '');
   134	  if (lines.length === 0) return { ok: false };
   135	  /** @type {any[]} */
   136	  const events = [];
   137	  for (const line of lines) {
   138	    let v;
   139	    try {
   140	      v = JSON.parse(line);
   141	    } catch {
   142	      return { ok: false };
   143	    }
   144	    if (v && typeof v === 'object' && v.schema === 'baton/event@1' && Array.isArray(v.events)) {
   145	      if (!v.events.every(isEventShaped)) return { ok: false };
   146	      events.push(...v.events);
   147	    } else if (isEventShaped(v)) {
   148	      events.push(v);
   149	    } else {
   150	      return { ok: false };
   151	    }
   152	  }
   153	  return { ok: true, raw: { schema: 'baton/event@1', events } };
   154	}

   156	/**
   157	 * `baton checkpoint` — mechanical checkpoint from a hook payload on stdin.
   158	 * Hook-safety rule: exit 0 on every soft failure so a checkpoint can never
   159	 * break the host harness; `--strict` opts into hard failures (CI).
   160	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
   161	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
   162	 */
   163	export async function cmdCheckpoint(args, io) {
   164	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { platform: 'string', model: 'string', trigger: 'string', debounce: 'string', strict: 'boolean', 'take-over': 'boolean' });
   165	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'checkpoint', parsed.error);
   166	  const flags = parsed.flags;
   167	  const strict = flags.strict === true;
   168	  const platform = typeof flags.platform === 'string' ? flags.platform : null;
   169	  if (!platform) return usageError(io, flags, 'checkpoint', '--platform <claude-code|codex|cursor> is required');
   170	  const pErr = platformError(platform);
   171	  if (pErr) return usageError(io, flags, 'checkpoint', pErr);
   172	
   173	  try {
   174	    return await run(flags, platform, io);
   175	  } catch (err) {
   176	    const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err);
   177	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'checkpoint-failed', msg } });
   178	    io.stderr.write(`baton checkpoint: ${msg}\n`);
   179	    return strict ? 1 : 0;
   180	  }
   181	}
   182	
   183	/**
   184	 * @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
   185	 * @param {string} platform
   186	 * @param {any} io
   187	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
   188	 */
   189	async function run(flags, platform, io) {
   190	  const strict = flags.strict === true;
   191	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
   192	  const paths = bundlePaths(root);
   193	  // The --json contract (gate-2 fix 11): exactly one envelope on stdout for
   194	  // every exit path — the exit code stays governed by hook-safety.
   195	  const finish = (/** @type {any} */ env, /** @type {number} */ code) => {
   196	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, env);
   197	    return code;
   198	  };
   199	
   200	  const parsed = parseStdin(typeof io.stdin === 'string' ? io.stdin : '');
   201	  if (!parsed.ok) {
   202	    const why = parsed.why ?? 'send one JSON value ({"schema":"baton/event@1","events":[…]}), a JSON array of events, or NDJSON event lines';
   203	    io.stderr.write(`baton checkpoint: stdin could not be parsed — ${why}\n`);
   204	    return finish({ ok: false, error: { code: 'bad-stdin', msg: why } }, strict ? 1 : 0);
   205	  }
   206	  const raw = parsed.raw;
   207	
   208	  const session = { host: io.host, pid: io.pid, startTime: io.startTime };
   209	  // The hook command declares which event it is (--trigger), so event identity
   210	  // never depends on parsing each harness's payload shape (Cursor's stop payload
   211	  // names the event nowhere normalize could find it).
   212	  const explicitEvent = typeof flags.trigger === 'string' ? flags.trigger : undefined;
   213	  const events = normalizeHookPayload(raw, platform, session, explicitEvent);
   214	  if (events.length === 0) return finish({ ok: true, data: { events: 0, rewritten: false } }, 0);
   215	
   216	  // Opt-in transcript tail (gate-2 fix 10): rides the journal as its own
   217	  // IMPORTANT event so the snapshot rewrite below persists it, and purge can
   218	  // strip it from every retained copy by its property name.
   219	  const tail = captureTranscriptTail(root, raw, io);
   220	  if (tail !== null) {
   221	    events.push({
   222	      type: 'transcript.set',
   223	      payload: { transcript: { capturedAt: io.now(), tail } },
   224	      source: platform,
   225	      sessionHint: events[0]?.sessionHint,
   226	      unstable: events[0]?.unstable ?? true,
   227	    });
   228	  }
   229	
   230	  const { bundle, warnings, unsafe } = loadBundle(root, io);
   231	  for (const w of warnings) io.stderr.write(`baton checkpoint: ${w}\n`);
   232	
   233	  // An unsafe managed tree (symlinked .handoff — gate-2 fix 6) is NOT "no
   234	  // bundle yet": seeding here would write through the link. Refuse everything;
   235	  // hook-safety keeps the refusal soft outside --strict.
   236	  if (unsafe === true) {
   237	    io.stderr.write('baton checkpoint: managed tree failed the symlink/realpath jail — nothing written\n');
   238	    return finish({ ok: false, error: { code: 'unsafe-tree', msg: warnings[0] ?? 'managed tree failed the jail check' } }, strict ? 1 : 0);
   239	  }
   240	
   241	  // Opt-in debounce (gate-2 major 14; Cursor afterFileEdit fires per edit):
   242	  // at most one real checkpoint per window, tracked by a per-platform stamp.
   243	  // The read-and-set runs UNDER the repo lock (gate-2 iter-2 M5) so two
   244	  // simultaneous afterFileEdit hooks can't both observe no stamp and both
   245	  // checkpoint — exactly one wins the window.
   246	  if (flags.debounce !== undefined) {
   247	    const seconds = Number(flags.debounce);
   248	    if (!Number.isFinite(seconds) || seconds <= 0) return usageError(io, flags, 'checkpoint', '--debounce <seconds> must be a positive number');
   249	    const stampPath = `${paths.logDir}/debounce-${platform}.json`;
   250	    const proceed = withLock(root, io, () => {
   251	      const stamp = safeReadJson(io.fs, stampPath);
   252	      if (stamp.ok && typeof stamp.value?.at === 'string' && Date.parse(io.now()) - Date.parse(stamp.value.at) < seconds * 1000) {
   253	        return false;
   254	      }
   255	      ensureDir(io.fs, paths.logDir);
   256	      atomicWriteJson(io.fs, stampPath, { at: io.now() });
   257	      return true;
   258	    });
   259	    if (!proceed) return finish({ ok: true, data: { events: 0, rewritten: false, debounced: true } }, 0);
   260	  }
   261	
   262	  let active = bundle;
   263	  let mustRewrite = false;
   264	
   265	  // Semantic isolation (plan §Concurrency): a stable foreign session or a
   266	  // mismatched origin platform must not merge into the active bundle. Checked
   267	  // before ANY write so a rejection leaves the whole tree untouched.
   268	  const originHint = active?.origin?.sessionHint;
   269	  const hasStableOwner = active !== null && typeof originHint === 'string' && originHint.length > 0 && active.origin.unstable !== true;
   270	  const stableIncoming = events.find(
   271	    (/** @type {any} */ e) => typeof e.sessionHint === 'string' && e.sessionHint.length > 0 && e.unstable === false,
   272	  );
   273	  const unstableIncoming = events.some((/** @type {any} */ e) => e.unstable === true);
   274	
   275	  if (hasStableOwner) {
   276	    // Foreign = a different stable session, OR any platform mismatch — the
   277	    // platform is provable even when the events carry no session hint (audit
   278	    // finding: a hint-less /baton:handoff narrative from claude-code silently
   279	    // merged into — then sealed — a codex-owned bundle, corrupting the origin
   280	    // the receive-side role avoidance keys on).
   281	    const incomingLabel = stableIncoming ? stableIncoming.sessionHint : '(no session hint)';
   282	    const foreign = stableIncoming
   283	      ? stableIncoming.sessionHint !== originHint || platform !== active.origin.platform
   284	      : platform !== active.origin.platform;
   285	    if (foreign && flags['take-over'] !== true) {
   286	      io.stderr.write(
   287	        `baton checkpoint: event from a foreign session/origin (${platform}/${incomingLabel} vs active ${active.origin.platform}/${originHint}) — rejected to keep sessions isolated; rerun with --take-over to archive the active bundle and start fresh\n`,
   288	      );
   289	      return finish({ ok: false, error: { code: 'foreign-session', msg: 'event from a foreign session/origin rejected; rerun with --take-over' } }, 0);
   290	    }
   291	    if (foreign) {
   292	      rotateJournal(root, 'takeover', io);
   293	      const fresh = emptyBundle(
   294	        { platform, model: typeof flags.model === 'string' ? flags.model : 'unknown', goal: active.task?.goal ?? 'unknown' },
   295	        io.now(),
   296	      );
   297	      if (stableIncoming) fresh.origin.sessionHint = stableIncoming.sessionHint;
   298	      writeSnapshot(root, fresh, io);
   299	      io.stderr.write(`baton checkpoint: took over — prior bundle archived to history/, fresh bundle owned by ${platform}/${incomingLabel}\n`);
   300	      active = fresh;
   301	      mustRewrite = true;
   302	    }
   303	  }
   304	
   305	  if (unstableIncoming) {
   306	    io.stderr.write('baton checkpoint: event carries an unstable (generated) session hint — ownership unverifiable; applied with this warning only\n');
   307	  }
   308	
   309	  if (active === null) {
   310	    active = emptyBundle(
   311	      { platform, model: typeof flags.model === 'string' ? flags.model : 'unknown', goal: 'unknown' },
   312	      io.now(),
   313	    );
   314	    writeSnapshot(root, active, io);
   315	    // Self-applying seed event (gate-2 iter-2 B6): a journal-only rebuild
   316	    // (snapshot + .bak both lost) must restore the bundle's identity, not the
   317	    // "unknown" placeholder resolveBase seeds. bundle.seed carries bundleId,
   318	    // origin, task, and generation and is applied on replay.
   319	    appendJournal(
   320	      root,
   321	      {
   322	        ts: io.now(),
   323	        type: 'bundle.seed',
   324	        payload: {
   325	          bundleId: active.bundleId,
   326	          generation: active.generation,
   327	          createdAt: active.createdAt,
   328	          origin: active.origin,
   329	          task: { goal: active.task.goal },
   330	          text: `[seed] auto-seeded a new bundle on ${platform} (model ${active.origin.model})`,
   331	        },
   332	        writerId: [platform, io.pid, 'seed'].join('-'),
   333	        dedupeKey: dedupeKey({ ts: io.now(), type: 'bundle.seed', bundleId: active.bundleId }),
   334	      },
   335	      io,
   336	    );
   337	    io.stderr.write('baton checkpoint: no bundle found — auto-seeded a new one at .handoff/bundle.json\n');
   338	    mustRewrite = true;
   339	  }

     1	import { normSep, joinNorm } from '../util/pathnorm.mjs';
     2	
     3	/**
     4	 * The --json contract: exactly one compact envelope line on stdout, nothing else.
     5	 * @param {any} io
     6	 * @param {{ok: boolean, data?: any, warnings?: any[], error?: {code: string, msg: string} | null}} env
     7	 */
     8	export function emitEnvelope(io, env) {
     9	  const full = { ok: env.ok, data: env.data ?? null, warnings: env.warnings ?? [], error: env.error ?? null };
    10	  io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(full) + '\n');
    11	}
    12	
    13	/**
    14	 * Centralized usage-error emission (gate-2 fix 11): with --json the envelope
    15	 * contract holds even for bad invocations; otherwise a one-line stderr
    16	 * message. Always returns the usage exit code 2.
    17	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
    18	 * @param {string} cmd @param {string} msg
    19	 * @returns {number}
    20	 */
    21	export function usageError(io, flags, cmd, msg) {
    22	  if (flags.json === true) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg } });
    23	  else io.stderr.write(`baton ${cmd}: ${msg}\n`);
    24	  return 2;
    25	}
    26	
    27	/**
    28	 * Repo-root discovery (plan §Root discovery): `--root` wins; else the nearest
    29	 * ancestor of io.cwd carrying `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`; else the
    30	 * nearest ancestor carrying `.git` (the repo toplevel — and a boundary the
    31	 * walk never crosses, so a nested repo resolves to itself); else io.cwd.
    32	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
    33	 * @returns {string}
    34	 */
    35	export function resolveRoot(io, flags) {
    36	  if (typeof flags.root === 'string' && flags.root.length > 0) return flags.root;
    37	  // Normalize separators up front (gate-2 iter-2 M2): a Windows cwd arrives
    38	  // backslash-separated, but Node fs accepts forward slashes, so the ascent
    39	  // and the marker checks work in one forward-slash space. The last separator
    40	  // is either kind; the loop stops at a drive/UNC or filesystem root.
    41	  const start = normSep(io.cwd);
    42	  let dir = start;
    43	  while (true) {
    44	    try {
    45	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.handoff')) || io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, 'baton.config.json'))) return dir;
    46	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.git'))) return dir; // repo toplevel; never walk past a .git boundary
    47	    } catch {
    48	      break;
    49	    }
    50	    const cut = dir.lastIndexOf('/');
    51	    if (cut < 0) break;
    52	    const parent = cut === 0 ? '/' : dir.slice(0, cut);
    53	    // At a drive-relative ceiling ("C:") examine the drive ROOT ("C:/") once —
    54	    // a repo can live directly at C:/ (iter-3 F6) — then stop.
    55	    if (/^[A-Za-z]:$/.test(parent)) {
    56	      const driveRoot = `${parent}/`;
    57	      if (driveRoot !== dir) {
    58	        dir = driveRoot;
    59	        continue;
    60	      }
    61	      break;
    62	    }
    63	    if (parent === dir) break;
    64	    dir = parent;
    65	  }
    66	  return start;
    67	}

    69	/** The supported platform enum, validated wherever a platform id is intake. */
    70	export const PLATFORMS = ['claude-code', 'codex', 'cursor'];
    71	
    72	/**
    73	 * Validate a platform id against the enum (surface-audit fold): a typo'd
    74	 * platform used to silently seed persistent state whose bogus origin then
    75	 * rejected the REAL platform's checkpoints as foreign.
    76	 * @param {string | boolean | undefined} value
    77	 * @returns {string | null} an error message, or null when valid/absent
    78	 */
    79	export function platformError(value) {
    80	  if (value === undefined) return null;
    81	  if (typeof value === 'string' && PLATFORMS.includes(value)) return null;
    82	  return `platform must be one of ${PLATFORMS.join('|')} (got ${JSON.stringify(value)})`;
    83	}
    84	
    85	/**
    86	 * Strict flag parser (surface-audit fold; docs/design/core.md §tooling always
    87	 * specified strict parsing). spec maps each flag name to 'string' | 'boolean';
    88	 * `json` and `root` are implied common flags. Errors — unknown flag, stray
    89	 * positional, missing string-flag value — return {error} so the command exits
    90	 * 2 instead of silently losing intent: the lenient parser let a stray token
    91	 * flip SAFETY flags off (checkpoint/doctor --strict, remap --native-only,
    92	 * receive --print-prompt all read fail-dangerous exit 0).
    93	 * @param {string[]} args
    94	 * @param {Record<string, 'string' | 'boolean'>} spec
    95	 * @returns {{flags: Record<string, string | boolean>, error?: string}}
    96	 */
    97	export function parseFlagsStrict(args, spec) {
    98	  /** @type {Record<string, 'string' | 'boolean'>} */
    99	  const full = { json: 'boolean', root: 'string', ...spec };
   100	  /** @type {Record<string, string | boolean>} */
   101	  const flags = {};
   102	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
   103	    const a = args[i];
   104	    if (!a.startsWith('--')) return { flags, error: `unexpected argument '${a}' — flags are --name [value]` };
   105	    const key = a.slice(2);
   106	    const kind = full[key];
   107	    if (kind === undefined) return { flags, error: `unknown flag --${key}` };
   108	    if (kind === 'boolean') {
   109	      // A following token that is not a flag would have been this flag's
   110	      // silent "value" under the lenient parser; reject it loudly.
   111	      const next = args[i + 1];
   112	      if (next !== undefined && !next.startsWith('--')) {
   113	        return { flags, error: `unexpected token '${next}' after --${key} (a bare flag takes no value)` };
   114	      }
   115	      flags[key] = true;
   116	      continue;
   117	    }
   118	    const next = args[i + 1];
   119	    // A string flag consumes the next token unless it is itself a KNOWN flag
   120	    // (so `--commit --origin x` errors instead of eating '--origin').
   121	    if (next === undefined || (next.startsWith('--') && full[next.slice(2)] !== undefined)) {
   122	      return { flags, error: `--${key} requires a value` };
   123	    }
   124	    flags[key] = next;
   125	    i += 1;
   126	  }
   127	  return { flags };
   128	}
   129	
   130	/**
   131	 * Minimal flag parser: `--key value` pairs (value = next token not starting
   132	 * with --), bare `--key` booleans, positionals collected in order.
   133	 * @param {string[]} args
   134	 * @returns {{flags: Record<string, string | boolean>, positionals: string[]}}
   135	 */
   136	export function parseFlags(args) {
   137	  /** @type {Record<string, string | boolean>} */
   138	  const flags = {};
   139	  /** @type {string[]} */
   140	  const positionals = [];
   141	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
   142	    const a = args[i];
   143	    if (a.startsWith('--')) {
   144	      const key = a.slice(2);
   145	      const next = args[i + 1];
   146	      if (next !== undefined && !next.startsWith('--')) {
   147	        flags[key] = next;
   148	        i += 1;
   149	      } else {
   150	        flags[key] = true;
   151	      }
   152	    } else {
   153	      positionals.push(a);
   154	    }
   155	  }
   156	  return { flags, positionals };
   157	}

   147	/**
   148	 * Version migration seam. Schema v1 is identity; anything else is unsupported.
   149	 * @param {any} obj
   150	 */
   151	export function migrate(obj) {
   152	  if (obj?.schema === BUNDLE_SCHEMA) return structuredClone(obj);
   153	  throw new Error(`unsupported bundle schema ${JSON.stringify(obj?.schema)}; this baton understands ${BUNDLE_SCHEMA}`);

One important integration point: receive tokens bind the current git snapshot and probe cache, and commit rejects drift. Any supervisor flow that finalizes, receives, and relaunches has to preserve exactly those bound inputs or explicitly re-prepare.

   377	    // Bounded git refresh on EVERY snapshot rewrite (gate-2 iter-2 M1): a
   378	    // mechanical checkpoint carries branch/HEAD/dirty. Capture git BEFORE the
   379	    // lock — it is async and must not straddle the lock — then do ONE atomic
   380	    // read-modify-write UNDER the lock (iter-5 A1): reload the bundle inside the
   381	    // lock, apply git, and write with the fencing token. The old code loaded
   382	    // outside the lock, awaited git (yielding the event loop), then wrote the
   383	    // stale bundle, so a concurrent --take-over during the await was clobbered
   384	    // (origin rolled back, the new owner's event lost). Reloading inside the
   385	    // lock guarantees the write is built from current state.
   386	    const git = await gitSnapshot({ execFile: io.execFile, cwd: root, fs: io.fs });
   387	    rewritten = withLock(root, io, (token) => {
   388	      const merged = loadBundle(root, io).bundle;
   389	      if (!merged) return false;
   390	      if (git !== null) {
   391	        merged.git = git;
   392	      } else {
   393	        // git refresh failed/timed out (iter-4 I2/F5): NEVER retain the prior
   394	        // snapshot — stale HEAD/dirty presented as current corrupts the
   395	        // receive-side evidence audit. Clear to explicit unavailable (null) and
   396	        // warn; a repo that was never git (already null) warns nothing.
   397	        if (merged.git !== null) {
   398	          io.stderr.write('baton checkpoint: git refresh failed — clearing stale git state (marked unavailable; receive re-derives it live)\n');
   399	        }
   400	        merged.git = null;
   401	      }
   402	      writeSnapshotIn(root, merged, io, token);
   403	      return true;
   404	    });
   405	  }
   406	  return finish({ ok: true, data: { events: events.length, rewritten } }, 0);
   407	}

     5	/** Audit note for a refused payload — visible in the decision log, never a throw. */
     6	const refusalNote = (/** @type {string} */ type, /** @type {any} */ detail) =>
     7	  `[${type} with invalid payload refused: ${JSON.stringify(detail).slice(0, 120)}]`;
     8	
     9	/**
    10	 * Pure event reducer. Returns a new bundle; never mutates the input.
    11	 * Idempotent: an event whose dedupeKey is still in the bundle's ring is a no-op.
    12	 * Unknown event types degrade to a decision-log note — a checkpoint must never
    13	 * fail because a newer adapter emitted something this core doesn't know — and
    14	 * journal payloads are untrusted (gate-2 fix 6): malformed shapes degrade to a
    15	 * refusal note and repository paths are jailed before storage.
    16	 * @param {any} bundle
    17	 * @param {{seq: number, ts: string, type: string, dedupeKey: string, writerId?: string, source?: string, payload: any}} event
    18	 */
    19	export function applyEvent(bundle, event) {
    20	  // Journal lines are untrusted (gate-2 iter-2 B4): a parseable null/number/
    21	  // string is a valid JSON line but not an event envelope — degrade to a
    22	  // no-op rather than crash replay. The store's load loop also pre-filters and
    23	  // warns; this is the last-line guard for any direct caller.
    24	  if (event === null || typeof event !== 'object' || Array.isArray(event)) return bundle;
    25	  if (bundle.dedupeRing.includes(event.dedupeKey)) return bundle;
    26	
    27	  const out = structuredClone(bundle);
    28	  const { seq, ts, type, payload = {} } = structuredClone(event);
    29	
    30	  switch (type) {
    31	    case 'decision': {
    32	      /** @type {{seq: number, ts: string, summary: any, detail?: any}} */
    33	      const entry = { seq, ts, summary: payload.summary };
    34	      if (payload.detail !== undefined) entry.detail = payload.detail;
    35	      out.decisions.push(entry);
    36	      break;
    37	    }
    38	    case 'note':
    39	      out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: payload.text });
    40	      // A structured StopFailure rate_limit note marks the open bundle
    41	      // limit-hit (gate-2 fix 3): the unsealed limit death must be visible to
    42	      // the next SessionStart on any platform, not buried in the decision log.
    43	      if (payload.structured?.kind === 'stop-failure' && payload.errorType === 'rate_limit' && out.handoff.status === 'open') {
    44	        out.handoff = { ...out.handoff, reason: out.handoff.reason ?? payload.text ?? null, reasonClass: 'usage-limit' };
    45	      }
    46	      break;
    47	    case 'plan.set':
    48	      if (Array.isArray(payload.steps) && payload.steps.every((/** @type {any} */ s) => s !== null && typeof s === 'object')) {
    49	        out.plan.steps = payload.steps;
    50	      } else {
    51	        out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: refusalNote('plan.set', payload?.steps) });
    52	      }
    53	      break;
    54	    case 'plan.step': {
    55	      if (payload === null || typeof payload !== 'object' || typeof payload.id !== 'string') {
    56	        out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: refusalNote('plan.step', payload) });
    57	        break;
    58	      }
    59	      const existing = out.plan.steps.find((/** @type {any} */ s) => s.id === payload.id);
    60	      if (existing) Object.assign(existing, payload);
    61	      else out.plan.steps.push(payload);
    62	      break;
    63	    }
    64	    case 'file.touch': {
    65	      const safePath = jailRelPath(payload?.path);
    66	      if (safePath === null) {
    67	        out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: refusalNote('file.touch', payload?.path) });
    68	        break;
    69	      }
    70	      const entry = { path: safePath, op: typeof payload.op === 'string' ? payload.op : 'touch', lastTs: ts };
    71	      const i = out.files.touched.findIndex((/** @type {any} */ f) => f.path === safePath);
    72	      if (i === -1) out.files.touched.push(entry);
    73	      else out.files.touched[i] = entry;
    74	      break;
    75	    }
    76	    case 'roles.remap': {
    77	      // The assignments object AND every value must be non-null objects (iter-3
    78	      // F3): a well-formed {planner: null} used to be stored verbatim and then
    79	      // crashed renderHandoffMd. Untrusted journal input degrades to a note.
    80	      const a = payload?.assignments;
    81	      const wellFormed =
    82	        a !== null &&
    83	        typeof a === 'object' &&
    84	        !Array.isArray(a) &&
    85	        Object.values(a).every((/** @type {any} */ v) => v !== null && typeof v === 'object' && !Array.isArray(v));
    86	      if (wellFormed) {
    87	        out.roles.assignments = a;
    88	      } else {
    89	        out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: refusalNote('roles.remap', a) });
    90	      }
    91	      break;
    92	    }
    93	    case 'task.update': {
    94	      /** @type {Record<string, any>} */
    95	      const patch = {};
    96	      if (typeof payload?.goal === 'string') patch.goal = payload.goal;
    97	      if (Array.isArray(payload?.constraints) && payload.constraints.every((/** @type {any} */ c) => typeof c === 'string')) {
    98	        patch.constraints = payload.constraints;
    99	      }
   100	      if (Array.isArray(payload?.acceptance)) patch.acceptance = payload.acceptance;
   101	      if (Object.keys(patch).length === 0) {
   102	        out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: refusalNote('task.update', payload) });
   103	        break;
   104	      }
   105	      out.task = { ...out.task, ...patch };
   106	      break;
   107	    }
   108	    case 'git.update':
   109	      if (payload === null || typeof payload === 'object') out.git = payload;
   110	      else out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: refusalNote('git.update', payload) });
   111	      break;
   112	    case 'bundle.seed':
   113	      // Self-applying identity (gate-2 iter-2 B6): the auto-seed emits this as
   114	      // the journal's first event so a journal-only rebuild (snapshot + .bak
   115	      // both gone) restores the bundle's identity/origin/task instead of the
   116	      // "unknown" placeholder resolveBase seeds. Replayed first (lowest seq),
   117	      // so later events layer on top; on normal replay its seq is below the
   118	      // snapshot's journalSeq and it is skipped.
   119	      if (payload && typeof payload === 'object') {
   120	        if (typeof payload.bundleId === 'string') out.bundleId = payload.bundleId;
   121	        if (typeof payload.generation === 'number') out.generation = payload.generation;
   122	        if (typeof payload.createdAt === 'string') out.createdAt = payload.createdAt;
   123	        // Restore identity ONLY (platform/model) — never sessionHint/unstable.
   124	        // Session ownership is adopted AFTER the seed (a snapshot-only mutation),
   125	        // so replaying the seed must not clobber a later-adopted hint back to
   126	        // its seed-time null (gate-2 iter-2: caught by the adoption test).
   127	        if (payload.origin && typeof payload.origin === 'object') {
   128	          if (typeof payload.origin.platform === 'string') out.origin.platform = payload.origin.platform;
   129	          if (typeof payload.origin.model === 'string') out.origin.model = payload.origin.model;
   130	        }
   131	        if (payload.task && typeof payload.task === 'object' && typeof payload.task.goal === 'string') {
   132	          out.task.goal = payload.task.goal;
   133	        }
   134	      }
   135	      break;
   136	    case 'transcript.set':
   137	      // Opt-in transcript tail (plan §Transcript policy): stored under the
   138	      // literal property name `transcript` so purge-transcript strips it from
   139	      // every retained copy. Render never includes it.
   140	      if (payload?.transcript !== null && typeof payload?.transcript === 'object' && typeof payload.transcript.tail === 'string') {
   141	        out.transcript = {
   142	          capturedAt: typeof payload.transcript.capturedAt === 'string' ? payload.transcript.capturedAt : ts,
   143	          tail: payload.transcript.tail,
   144	        };
   145	      } else {
   146	        out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: refusalNote('transcript.set', 'malformed payload') });
   147	      }
   148	      break;
   149	    default:
   150	      out.decisions.push({ seq, ts, summary: `[unhandled event type '${type}' recorded as note]` });
   151	      break;
   152	  }
   153	
   154	  out.updatedAt = ts;
   155	  // Harden against foreign/keyless entries (gate-2 fix): a missing seq must
   156	  // never poison journalSeq to NaN, and an absent dedupeKey must never enter
   157	  // the ring (undefined in the ring dedupes every later keyless event).
   158	  if (typeof seq === 'number' && Number.isFinite(seq)) out.journalSeq = Math.max(out.journalSeq, seq);
   159	  if (typeof event.dedupeKey === 'string' && event.dedupeKey.length > 0) {
   160	    out.dedupeRing.push(event.dedupeKey);
   161	    if (out.dedupeRing.length > RING_CAP) out.dedupeRing.splice(0, out.dedupeRing.length - RING_CAP);
   162	  }
   163	
   164	  return out;
   165	}

    29	  codex: { floor: '0.144', label: '0.144' }, // lifecycle hooks landed in 0.144

    35	/** @param {any} text @returns {string | null} */
    36	const parseVersion = (text) => String(text).match(/\d+(?:\.\d+)+/)?.[0] ?? null;
    37	
    38	/** Numeric segment-wise version compare: negative when a < b. */
    39	function cmpVersions(/** @type {string} */ a, /** @type {string} */ b) {
    40	  const as = a.split('.').map(Number);
    41	  const bs = b.split('.').map(Number);
    42	  for (let i = 0; i < Math.max(as.length, bs.length); i++) {
    43	    const d = (as[i] ?? 0) - (bs[i] ?? 0);
    44	    if (d !== 0) return d < 0 ? -1 : 1;
    45	  }
    46	  return 0;
    47	}

    49	/** @param {string} platform @param {any} io @returns {Promise<string | null>} */
    50	async function versionOf(platform, io) {
    51	  try {
    52	    const r = await io.execFile(VERSION_BINS[platform], ['--version'], { timeout: PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS });
    53	    return parseVersion(`${r.stdout ?? ''} ${r.stderr ?? ''}`);
    54	  } catch {
    55	    return null;
    56	  }
    57	}

    59	/** @param {string} platform @param {string | null} v @returns {{id: string, ok: boolean, detail: string}} */
    60	function versionCheck(platform, v) {
    61	  const id = `version-${platform}`;
    62	  if (v === null) {
    63	    return { id, ok: true, detail: `${VERSION_BINS[platform]} version unverifiable (not installed or --version unsupported) — floor not enforced` };
    64	  }
    65	  const f = FLOORS[platform];
    66	  if (f.floor && cmpVersions(v, f.floor) < 0) {
    67	    return { id, ok: false, detail: `${platform} ${v} is below the supported floor ${f.label} — lifecycle hooks unavailable; the adapter degrades to protocol-only (Tier C)` };
    68	  }
    69	  if (cmpVersions(v, TESTED[platform]) > 0) {
    70	    return { id, ok: true, detail: `${platform} ${v} is newer than tested (${TESTED[platform]}) — untested; signature drift is absorbed by the overlay table` };
    71	  }
    72	  return { id, ok: true, detail: `${platform} ${v} (tested ${TESTED[platform]})` };
    73	}

    75	/**
    76	 * Recursively collect every `command`/`commandWindows` STRING under a hook
    77	 * definition (codex nests hooks[].hooks[].command; cursor is [].command). Only
    78	 * real command fields — never matchers, comments, or serialized metadata — so
    79	 * enablement keys on an actual invocation (iter-5 A3).
    80	 * @param {any} def @returns {string[]}
    81	 */
    82	function hookCommands(def) {
    83	  /** @type {string[]} */
    84	  const out = [];
    85	  const walk = (/** @type {any} */ node) => {
    86	    if (Array.isArray(node)) {
    87	      for (const n of node) walk(n);
    88	    } else if (node && typeof node === 'object') {
    89	      if (typeof node.command === 'string') out.push(node.command);
    90	      if (typeof node.commandWindows === 'string') out.push(node.commandWindows);
    91	      if (Array.isArray(node.hooks)) walk(node.hooks);
    92	    }
    93	  };
    94	  walk(def);
    95	  return out;
    96	}

    98	/**
    99	 * Codex/Cursor hook surface: installed / enabled / trusted / observed-executing.
   100	 * `installed` = the hooks file references baton at all; `enabled` = the
   101	 * mechanical-checkpoint hook (the `checkpointEvent` key: codex `Stop`, cursor
   102	 * `stop`) declares a `baton checkpoint` COMMAND (iter-5 A3); `trusted` is not
   103	 * externally verifiable → 'unknown'; `observed` = a platform-sourced checkpoint
   104	 * whose payload.trigger is the checkpointEvent (the per-turn Stop canary). The
   105	 * ≤1-turn fidelity claim is gated on enabled AND observed.
   106	 * @param {any} io @param {string} root @param {any} p bundlePaths
   107	 * @param {string} platform @param {string} relPath @param {string} checkpointEvent @param {string} trustNote
   108	 */
   109	function hookSurfaceCheck(io, root, p, platform, relPath, checkpointEvent, trustNote) {
   110	  const id = `${platform}-hooks`;
   111	  let text = null;
   112	  try {
   113	    text = io.fs.readFileSync(`${root}/${relPath}`, 'utf8');
   114	  } catch {
   115	    text = null;
   116	  }
   117	  const installed = text !== null && text.includes('baton');
   118	  if (!installed) {
   119	    return {
   120	      id,
   121	      ok: true,
   122	      states: { installed: false, enabled: false, trusted: 'unknown', observed: false },
   123	      detail: `not installed — run 'baton init --${platform}' to write ${relPath}`,
   124	    };
   125	  }
   126	  // enabled: the checkpoint hook itself is declared with a `baton checkpoint`
   127	  // COMMAND (iter-5 A3) — inspect the actual command/commandWindows fields, not
   128	  // the serialized blob (a matcher/comment mentioning baton must not count). A
   129	  // file that references baton only in a session-start hook is installed but NOT
   130	  // checkpoint-enabled.
   131	  let enabled = false;
   132	  try {
   133	    const cfg = JSON.parse(/** @type {string} */ (text));
   134	    // Match the bare `baton checkpoint` form AND the absolute
   135	    // `"<node>" "<…/baton.mjs>" checkpoint` form init now writes (the entry
   136	    // script may be quote-terminated: `baton.mjs" checkpoint`).
   137	    enabled = hookCommands(cfg?.hooks?.[checkpointEvent]).some((c) => /\bbaton(?:\.mjs)?["']?\s+checkpoint\b/.test(c));
   138	  } catch {
   139	    enabled = false;
   140	  }
   141	  // observed: a mechanical checkpoint from THIS platform's CHECKPOINT hook is
   142	  // recorded — the journal entry must be platform-sourced AND carry
   143	  // payload.trigger === checkpointEvent (iter-5 A3). normalize stamps the hook
   144	  // event name as payload.trigger, so a SessionStart/PreCompact/triggerless
   145	  // entry no longer masquerades as the per-turn Stop canary.
   146	  let observed = false;
   147	  try {
   148	    for (const e of readAllTolerant(io.fs, p.journal).entries) {
   149	      const mine = String(e?.writerId ?? '').startsWith(`${platform}-`) || e?.source === platform;
   150	      if (mine && e?.payload?.trigger === checkpointEvent) {
   151	        observed = true;
   152	        break;
   153	      }
   154	    }
   155	  } catch {
   156	    observed = false;
   157	  }
   158	  const states = { installed: true, enabled, trusted: 'unknown', observed };
   159	  // The ≤1-turn claim needs the checkpoint hook DECLARED (enabled) AND OBSERVED —
   160	  // neither alone suffices.
   161	  const fidelityHolds = enabled && observed;
   162	  const enabledNote = enabled ? `${checkpointEvent} checkpoint hook enabled` : `${checkpointEvent} checkpoint hook NOT declared (enabled=false)`;
   163	  return {
   164	    id,
   165	    ok: true,
   166	    states,
   167	    detail: fidelityHolds
   168	      ? `installed, ${enabledNote}; trusted=unknown (not externally verifiable); execution OBSERVED (canary) — the ≤1-turn mechanical-staleness claim holds`
   169	      : `installed, ${enabledNote}; trusted=unknown — ${trustNote}; ${observed ? 'execution observed but' : 'execution not yet observed —'} the ≤1-turn fidelity claim is gated on the ${checkpointEvent} canary + enablement`,
   170	  };
   171	}

   173	// Noninteractive status probes per platform (docs/design/platform-notes.md CLIs).
   174	// capOnSuccess caps what a clean exit PROVES (gate-2 iter-2 M3): `claude
   175	// --version` proves the binary is INSTALLED, not that the server was reached or
   176	// that auth is valid, so its capability is capped at "installed". The codex/
   177	// cursor probes are auth-checking status commands, so a clean exit legitimately
   178	// proves "reachable".
   179	const PROBES = [
   180	  { platform: 'claude-code', bin: 'claude', args: ['--version'], capOnSuccess: 'installed' },
   181	  { platform: 'codex', bin: 'codex', args: ['login', 'status'], capOnSuccess: 'reachable' },
   182	  { platform: 'cursor', bin: 'cursor-agent', args: ['status'], capOnSuccess: 'reachable' },
   183	];

   185	// Word-boundary-safe AI-attribution trailer patterns (plan §Attribution guard).
   186	const TRAILER_PATTERNS = [
   187	  /co-authored-by:[^\n]*\b(claude|gpt|copilot|codex)\b/i,

   190	];
   191	
   192	/** @param {any} io @param {string} path @returns {string | null} */
   193	function readOrNull(io, path) {
   194	  try {
   195	    return io.fs.readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
   196	  } catch {
   197	    return null;
   198	  }
   199	}
   200	
   201	/**
   202	 * Classify one probe invocation per the two-dimension record: capability is
   203	 * the ordered ladder verified as far as the evidence goes; outcome is what the
   204	 * probe itself did. A server-issued limit response proves authentication.
   205	 * @param {{platform: string, bin: string, args: string[], capOnSuccess?: string}} probe @param {any} io @param {any} table
   206	 */
   207	async function runProbe(probe, io, table) {
   208	  try {
   209	    await io.execFile(probe.bin, probe.args, { timeout: PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS });
   210	    return { platform: probe.platform, capability: probe.capOnSuccess ?? 'reachable', outcome: 'ok' };
   211	  } catch (err) {
   212	    const e = /** @type {any} */ (err);
   213	    if (e?.code === 'ENOENT') return { platform: probe.platform, capability: null, outcome: 'error' };
   214	    if (e?.killed === true) return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'timeout' };
   215	    const text = `${e?.stdout ?? ''}\n${e?.stderr ?? ''}`;
   216	    if (table && classify({ text, exitCode: 1, platform: probe.platform, table, structured: null }).class === 'usage-limit') {
   217	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'rate-limited' };
   218	    }
   219	    if (/authenticated as|logged in as/i.test(text) && /unreachable|ECONN|offline|network/i.test(text)) {
   220	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'error' };
   221	    }
   222	    if (/not logged in|log ?in first|unauthorized|authenticat/i.test(text)) {
   223	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'error' };
   224	    }
   225	    return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'error' };
   226	  }
   227	}
   228	
   229	/** @param {any} io @param {string} root @returns {Promise<{id: string, ok: boolean, detail?: string}>} */
   230	async function gitGuardCheck(io, root) {
   231	  let log;
   232	  try {
   233	    log = await io.execFile('git', ['log', '-n', String(GUARD_COMMIT_BOUND), '--format=%h %s%n%n%b'], { cwd: root, timeout: 5000 });
   234	  } catch {
   235	    return { id: 'git-guard', ok: true, detail: 'git unavailable — commit scan skipped' };
   236	  }
   237	  // Chunks start at a line beginning with an abbreviated sha + space.
   238	  const chunks = log.stdout.split(/^(?=[0-9a-f]{7,40} )/m).filter((/** @type {string} */ c) => c.trim() !== '');
   239	  for (const chunk of chunks) {
   240	    if (TRAILER_PATTERNS.some((re) => re.test(chunk))) {
   241	      const sha = chunk.slice(0, chunk.indexOf(' '));
   242	      return {
   243	        id: 'git-guard',
   244	        ok: false,
   245	        detail: `commit ${sha} carries an AI attribution trailer — rewrite it before publishing (git rebase -i / git commit --amend)`,
   246	      };
   247	    }
   248	  }
   249	  return { id: 'git-guard', ok: true, detail: `no AI trailers in the last ${GUARD_COMMIT_BOUND} commits` };
   250	}
   251	
   252	/**
   253	 * `baton doctor` — machine health report: attribution settings, git-guard
   254	 * trailer scan, journal size, filesystem boundary, and per-platform probe
   255	 * records ({capability, outcome}, 3 s bound, 15-min cache). Report-only by
   256	 * default; `--strict` turns any failing check into exit 1.
   257	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
   258	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
   259	 */
   260	export async function cmdDoctor(args, io) {
   261	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { strict: 'boolean' });
   262	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'doctor', parsed.error);
   263	  const flags = parsed.flags;
   264	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
   265	  const p = bundlePaths(root);
   266	  /** @type {{id: string, ok: boolean, detail?: string}[]} */
   267	  const checks = [];
   268	
   269	  // Attribution settings: repo-level first, then the user's global file.
   270	  const settingsText = readOrNull(io, `${root}/.claude/settings.json`) ?? readOrNull(io, `${io.env?.HOME ?? ''}/.claude/settings.json`);
   271	  let attrOk = false;
   272	  try {
   273	    const s = settingsText === null ? null : JSON.parse(settingsText);
   274	    attrOk = s?.attribution?.commit === '' && s?.attribution?.pr === '';
   275	  } catch {
   276	    attrOk = false;
   277	  }
   278	  checks.push({
   279	    id: 'attribution-settings',
   280	    ok: attrOk,
   281	    detail: attrOk ? 'attribution.commit and attribution.pr are ""' : 'set {"attribution":{"commit":"","pr":""}} in .claude/settings.json (baton init does this)',
   282	  });
   283	
   284	  checks.push(await gitGuardCheck(io, root));
   285	
   286	  // .handoff gitignore coverage (audit finding): doctor itself writes the
   287	  // probe cache into .handoff/log/, so running doctor BEFORE init in a clean
   288	  // repo leaves .handoff/ committable. Flag the gap whenever the tree exists
   289	  // without ignore coverage.
   290	  {
   291	    let handoffExists = false;
   292	    try {
   293	      handoffExists = io.fs.statSync(`${root}/.handoff`).isDirectory();
   294	    } catch {
   295	      handoffExists = false;
   296	    }
   297	    const covered = !ensureIgnoreLine(readOrNull(io, `${root}/.gitignore`), '.handoff/').changed;
   298	    checks.push({
   299	      id: 'handoff-ignored',
   300	      ok: !handoffExists || covered,
   301	      detail:
   302	        !handoffExists || covered
   303	          ? handoffExists
   304	            ? '.gitignore covers .handoff/'
   305	            : 'no .handoff/ tree yet'
   306	          : '.handoff/ exists but .gitignore does not cover it — run baton init (never commit .handoff/)',
   307	    });
   308	  }
   309	
   310	  const journalText = readOrNull(io, p.journal);
   311	  const journalBytes = journalText === null ? 0 : Buffer.byteLength(journalText);
   312	  checks.push({
   313	    id: 'journal-size',
   314	    ok: journalBytes <= JOURNAL_WARN_BYTES,
   315	    detail: journalBytes > JOURNAL_WARN_BYTES ? `journal is ${(journalBytes / 1048576).toFixed(1)} MB (> 5 MB) — finalize to rotate it` : `${journalBytes} bytes`,
   316	  });
   317	
   318	
   319	  // Platform probes + version probes + mount detection: every non-git child
   320	  // invocation rides the same 15-min cache in the state dir.
   321	  const cachePath = `${p.logDir}/probe-cache.json`;
   322	  const cached = safeReadJson(io.fs, cachePath);
   323	  /** @type {any[]} */
   324	  let platforms;
   325	  /** @type {Record<string, string | null>} */
   326	  let versions;
   327	  /** @type {string | null} */
   328	  let mountSource;
   329	  if (cached.ok && typeof cached.value?.at === 'string' && Date.parse(io.now()) - Date.parse(cached.value.at) < PROBE_CACHE_MS && Array.isArray(cached.value.records)) {
   330	    platforms = cached.value.records;
   331	    versions = cached.value.versions ?? {};
   332	    mountSource = cached.value.mount ?? null;
   333	  } else {
   334	    /** @type {any} */
   335	    let table = null;
   336	    try {
   337	      // Packaged data, not repo state: read through the real fs so probes can
   338	      // classify limit responses regardless of the injected io.fs contents.
   339	      table = loadSignatures({ builtinPath: BUILTIN_SIGNATURES }, { fs: realFs });
   340	    } catch {
   341	      table = null;
   342	    }
   343	    platforms = [];
   344	    for (const probe of PROBES) platforms.push(await runProbe(probe, io, table));
   345	    versions = {};
   346	    for (const platform of Object.keys(VERSION_BINS)) versions[platform] = await versionOf(platform, io);
   347	    mountSource = null;
   348	    try {
   349	      const df = await io.execFile('df', ['-P', root], { timeout: 2000 });
   350	      mountSource = ((df.stdout ?? '').split('\n')[1] ?? '').trim().split(/\s+/)[0] || null;
   351	    } catch {
   352	      mountSource = null;
   353	    }
   354	    // Jail before writing the probe cache (iter-3 F4): the cache rides
   355	    // .handoff/log, so a symlinked .handoff or log dir must never redirect the
   356	    // write outside the repository (threat model). Diagnostics are best-effort —
   357	    // on an unsafe tree we simply skip caching; the atomic write already
   358	    // prevents a torn cache under concurrent doctors.
   359	    if (checkHandoffTree(root, io).ok) {
   360	      ensureDir(io.fs, p.logDir);

./reviews/2026-07-12-gate-2-final/reviewer-a/iteration-04/raw-output.txt:1349:62:**Root discovery & degraded modes** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): repo root = nearest ancestor containing `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`, else `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (worktree-aware: linked worktrees resolve to their own toplevel, shared state stays per-worktree), else cwd — `--root <path>` overrides; nested/monorepo launches therefore resolve to the same bundle deterministically, but **discovery never crosses the boundary of a nested independent git repository** (a `.git` between cwd and a candidate root stops the walk) unless `--root` says otherwise. All git invocations carry a 5 s timeout (configurable); timeout → git section marked `unavailable`, never a hang.

     1	# Review artifacts
     2	
     3	Every review gate leaves an auditable trail here. Layout:
     4	
     5	```
     6	reviews/
     7	  _templates/               # prompt templates per gate type
     8	  <task-id>/                # YYYY-MM-DD-slug
     9	    gate-1-plan/
    10	      iteration-NN/{prompt.md, verdict.md}
    11	    subtask-<name>-tests/
    12	      iteration-NN/{prompt.md, verdict.md}
    13	    gate-2-final/
    14	      reviewer-a/iteration-NN/{prompt.md, verdict.md}
    15	      reviewer-b/iteration-NN/{prompt.md, verdict.md}
    16	      findings.md           # collated blocking / non-blocking items
    17	```
    18	
    19	Verdicts: `APPROVED` / `APPROVED_WITH_NOTES` / `BLOCKED`. Notes are folded in; BLOCKED means revise and re-review.
    20	
    21	Every `verdict.md` starts with a header block:
    22	
    23	```
    24	role: <plan-reviewer | test-verifier | final-reviewer-a | final-reviewer-b>
    25	model: <concrete model @ effort actually used>
    26	harness: <how it was invoked>
    27	date: <YYYY-MM-DD>
    28	verdict: <APPROVED | APPROVED_WITH_NOTES | BLOCKED>
    29	degraded: <none | single-vendor | model-fallback | ...>
    30	```
    31	
    32	Iteration budget: a gate loops at most **5** times. After the 5th iteration the gate closes; outstanding findings escalate to Saksham for a decision.

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# Core engine design — module APIs and test lists
     2	
     3	Authoritative plan: `docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md` (Gate-1 approved). Where this doc and the plan differ, **the plan wins** — the Concurrency, Receive-transaction, Journal-rotation, Lock-model, and Detector sections were amended across Gate-1 iterations 1–5 and are specified there in full.
     4	
     5	## Language and tooling
     6	
     7	Plain ESM JavaScript (`.mjs`) with JSDoc types, checked by `tsc --noEmit --checkJs` (dev-only). No build step: hooks invoke source directly. Zero runtime dependencies; strict spec-based flag parsing (`parseFlagsStrict` in commands/shared.mjs — unknown flags, stray positionals, and missing string-flag values are exit-2 usage errors, so a garbled safety flag can never silently lose intent); `node:test` with a small golden-file helper (`UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 npm test` regenerates). Node >= 20.
     8	
     9	Testability backbone: every command's `run()` takes an injected `io` object — `{ cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now }`. Pure logic (reducers, renderers, resolvers, classifiers) takes plain data and returns plain data. Unit tests run over an in-memory fs fake (`tests/helpers/memfs.mjs`); a thin integration layer uses `fs.mkdtemp` on the real fs.
    10	
    11	## File tree
    12	
    13	```
    14	core/
    15	├── bin/baton.mjs             # shebang; delegates to src/cli.mjs
    16	├── src/
    17	│   ├── cli.mjs               # routing, --json envelope, exit-code contract
    18	│   ├── commands/             # checkpoint, finalize, detect, remap, receive,
    19	│   │                         #   init, doctor, status, purge-transcript, recover
    20	│   ├── bundle/
    21	│   │   ├── schema.mjs        # emptyBundle, validateBundle, migrate
    22	│   │   ├── store.mjs         # load/write .handoff/, journal, recovery, rotation
    23	│   │   ├── lock.mjs          # operation-scoped lock (see plan §Lock model)
    24	│   │   ├── merge.mjs         # applyEvent reducer (pure)
    25	│   │   ├── normalize.mjs     # hook payload -> baton/event@1 extractors
    26	│   │   ├── compact.mjs       # deterministic size-budget enforcement (pure)
    27	│   │   └── render.mjs        # bundle -> HANDOFF.md (pure)
    28	│   ├── detect/
    29	│   │   ├── signatures.mjs    # load + validate + overlay signature tables
    30	│   │   └── classifier.mjs    # classify(text, exitCode, platform) (pure)
    31	│   ├── roles/
    32	│   │   ├── matrix.mjs        # load/validate baton.config.json
    33	│   │   └── resolve.mjs       # remap resolver (pure, explainable)
    34	│   ├── receive/
    35	│   │   ├── prompt.mjs        # resume-prompt renderer (pure, <=2500 chars)
    36	│   │   └── txn.mjs           # prepare/commit transaction + receipt tokens
    37	│   ├── scaffold/
    38	│   │   ├── plan.mjs          # planInit -> Action[]; applyActions
    39	│   │   ├── gitignore.mjs     # ensure-line (pure text transform)
    40	│   │   ├── managed-block.mjs # <!-- baton:begin/end --> merges
    41	│   │   └── attribution.mjs   # .claude/settings.json deep-merge (pure)
    42	│   ├── git/
    43	│   │   ├── snapshot.mjs      # branch/HEAD/dirty + content digest via execFile
    44	│   │   └── guard.mjs         # scan recent commits for AI trailers
    45	│   └── util/
    46	│       ├── fsx.mjs           # atomicWriteJson, safeReadJson, backupThenWrite
    47	│       ├── jsonl.mjs         # appendEntry, readAllTolerant
    48	│       └── ids.mjs           # bundleId, event dedupeKey (sha256 canonical)
    49	└── data/signatures.v1.json   # versioned limit-signature table (data, not code)
    50	```
    51	
    52	Tests mirror this: `tests/{helpers,unit,commands,invariants,integration}/`.
    53	
    54	## Bundle schema (`baton/bundle@1`)
    55	
    56	`bundle.json` fields: `schema`, `bundleId`, `generation`, `createdAt/updatedAt`, `origin {platform, model, sessionHint, unstable}`, `task {goal, constraints[], acceptance[]}`, `plan.steps[] {id, title, status: pending|active|done|blocked, note}`, `decisions[] {seq, ts, summary, detail(capped)}`, `files {touched[] {path, op, lastTs}, truncated}`, `roles.assignments`, `git {branch, headSha, dirty, dirtySummary[], contentDigest, summaryTruncated} | null`, `handoff {status: open|sealed|received, reason, reasonClass, toPlatformHint, finalizedAt, receive_log[]}`, `journalSeq`, `compaction {droppedDecisions, note}`, `dedupeRing: string[]` (merge idempotency ring, capped 500, oldest evicted), transcript field (opt-in, redacted).
    57	
    58	`journal.ndjson` entry: `{seq, ts, type, dedupeKey, writerId, source, payload}`. Types: `decision | plan.set | plan.step | file.touch | note | roles.remap | task.update | git.update`. Every type is self-applicable for replay over `emptyBundle()`.
    59	
    60	Size budget (pure `compact.mjs`): decisions capped first-20 + last-150 + marker; `detail` <= 2000 chars; `files.touched` <= 300 (LRU by `lastTs`, sets `files.truncated: true`); `git.dirtySummary` <= 100 lines (sets `git.summaryTruncated: true`; skipped when `git` is null). Deterministic truncation only — no LLM summarization.
    61	
    62	## Module APIs
    63	
    64	```js
    65	// bundle/schema.mjs
    66	emptyBundle({platform, model, goal}, nowIso) -> Bundle
    67	validateBundle(obj) -> {ok, errors: [{path, msg}]}    // hand-rolled, no deps
    68	migrate(obj) -> Bundle                                 // identity in v1
    69	
    70	// bundle/store.mjs  (all take io)
    71	bundlePaths(root) -> {dir, snapshot, bak, journal, handoffMd, historyDir, lockDir, logDir}
    72	loadBundle(root, io) -> {bundle, warnings[]}           // snapshot + replay + recovery
    73	writeSnapshot(root, bundle, io) -> void                // bak -> tmp -> rename, under lock
    74	appendJournal(root, entry, io) -> seq                  // seq allocated under lock
    75	rotateJournal(root, kind, io) -> historyPath           // marker-based, crash-safe (plan §rotation)
    76	
    77	// bundle/lock.mjs — operation-scoped; see plan §Lock model for takeover rules
    78	withLock(root, io, fn) -> result                       // acquire -> fn -> release
    79	recoverLock(root, io, {force}) -> {recovered, refusedReason}
    80	
    81	// bundle/merge.mjs (pure)
    82	applyEvent(bundle, event) -> Bundle                    // idempotent via dedupeKey ring (500)
    83	
    84	// bundle/normalize.mjs
    85	normalizeHookPayload(raw, platform) -> Event[]         // never throws; unknowns -> note
    86	
    87	// bundle/render.mjs (pure)
    88	renderHandoffMd(bundle) -> string                      // golden-tested, no AI-credit strings
    89	
    90	// detect/
    91	loadSignatures({builtinPath, overlayPath}, io) -> Table // validates, lints regexes
    92	classify({text, exitCode, platform, table, structured}) ->
    93	  {class, signatureId, confidence, resetHint, tried?}   // exit-code rule: plan §detector
    94	
    95	// roles/
    96	loadConfig(cwd, io) -> {config, errors[]}
    97	resolveRoles({config, to, avoid, nativeOnly, probes}) ->
    98	  {assignments: {[role]: {platform, model, mode, chainIndex, skipped[]}}, notes[]}
    99	
   100	// receive/txn.mjs — see plan §Concurrency for full transition semantics
   101	prepare(root, {platform, origin, reason}, io) -> {token, prompt, assignments, warnings[]}
   102	commit(root, token, io) -> {adopted, generation, archivedTo}   // rejects bound-input drift
   103	
   104	// scaffold/
   105	planInit(cwd, opts, io) -> Action[]                    // {path, kind, reason, preview}
   106	applyActions(actions, io) -> Applied[]
   107	mergeAttribution(existingJsonText|null, {force}) -> {text, changed, warnings} | {error}
   108	
   109	// git/
   110	snapshot({execFile, cwd}) -> {branch, headSha, dirty, dirtySummary, contentDigest} // 5s timeout
   111	scanCommitsForAiTrailers({execFile, cwd, limit: 50}) -> [{sha, line, pattern}]
   112	```
   113	
   114	## Per-module test lists
   115	
   116	- **fsx**: atomic write visible-only-after-rename; `.bak` rotation; leftover `.tmp` ignored/cleaned; `safeReadJson` on garbage; Windows rename-retry (EPERM).
   117	- **jsonl/ids**: append+read roundtrip; torn last line dropped with warning; seq monotonic; dedupeKey stable across key order.
   118	- **schema**: empty bundle validates; pathed error per missing field; unknown fields tolerated; `@2` rejected by migrate.
   119	- **merge**: each event type mutates its section; double-apply = identical bundle; unknown type degrades to note; seq ordering.
   120	- **compact**: over-budget collapse deterministic (byte-identical); under-budget identity; truncation markers.
   121	- **store**: snapshot+replay equals pure reduction; corrupt snapshot -> `.bak` recovery; both corrupt -> journal rebuild; torn tail; marker-based rotation roll-forward/back; kill-mid-write sim.
   122	- **lock**: mutual exclusion; provably-dead takeover; live same-host owner refused (incl. `--force`); torn metadata refused; pause-after-final-fence-check; crash between creation and metadata publication.
   123	- **normalize**: `baton/event@1` passthrough; per-harness fixture extraction (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor payloads); malformed-input fuzz never throws.
   124	- **render / receive-prompt**: goldens (minimal, mid-session, finalized, stale, degraded-role); char caps; attribution invariant; determinism.
   125	- **signatures/classifier**: verbatim positive fixtures per platform; negative/near-miss corpus; ANSI stripping; precedence; reset-hint extraction; exit-code interplay matrix; non-limit failing-process fixtures; overlay override; bad-regex rejection; time guard.
   126	- **matrix/resolve**: config validation (pathed errors); chain walking with avoid/disabled/rate-limited skips; native/delegated/forced-default/unavailable; probe-eligibility rules; determinism.
   127	- **txn**: token binds journalSeq + intake + config digest + session + probe snapshot + content-sensitive git digest; one rejection test per bound input; `sealed->received` and `open->received` (degraded seal); ownership adoption + new generation; chained A->B->C; competing receivers; double-commit.
   128	- **scaffold**: gitignore variants (covering pattern no-op, CRLF, trailing newline, idempotent double-run); managed-block replace/append/corrupt-marker-refuse with outside-bytes invariance; attribution merge (absent/present/non-empty/malformed-refuse); `--dry-run` zero writes; real-fs `mkdtemp` + `git init` integration.
   129	- **git**: snapshot on clean/dirty/detached/not-a-repo/timeout; guard trailer fixtures incl. word-boundary false-positive ("Claudette Smith" not flagged).
   130	- **doctor**: check aggregation; `--strict` exit; probe states; version floors; network-FS warning.
   131	- **purge-transcript**: whole-tree secret removal (snapshot, bak, journal, history, log); interruption resume via marker; packaged-bin invocation.
   132	- **invariants**: no AI-credit strings in any rendered output, golden, or template.
   133	- **integration**: e2e failover (init -> 25 checkpoints -> kill-mid-write -> finalize usage-limit -> receive on second platform -> golden prompt); chained A->B->C; six-direction contract matrix; cli-spawn exit codes and `--json` stdout purity.
   134	
   135	## CLI contract
   136	
   137	Commands: `checkpoint`, `finalize`, `detect`, `remap`, `receive`, `init`, `doctor`, `status`, `purge-transcript`, `recover`, `session-start`. Exit codes: `0` ok · `1` command failure · `2` usage error · `10` usage-limit · `11` throttle · `12` auth · `13` other-error. `--json`: exactly one `{ok, data, warnings, error}` envelope on stdout, diagnostics on stderr. Checkpoint soft-fails to exit 0 in hook contexts (`--strict` opts out). `session-start` is the cheap adapter SessionStart gate: soft paths always exit 0 and its BARE stdout is harness-shaped context (not an envelope); with `--json` it honors the envelope contract (`data: {pending, shaped}`). `wrap` is reserved, not implemented in v1.

    11	## Claude Code
    12	
    13	- Hook events include `Stop`, `StopFailure`, `PreCompact`, `PostCompact`, `SessionStart`, `SessionEnd`, `PostToolUseFailure`, and others.
    14	- **`StopFailure`** fires when a turn ends due to an API error; output and exit code are ignored (observability-only). Matcher filters by error type. **Enum snapshot (2026-07-11, code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks):** `rate_limit`, `overloaded`, `authentication_failed`, `oauth_org_not_allowed`, `billing_error`, `invalid_request`, `model_not_found`, `server_error`, `max_output_tokens`, `unknown`. Note: a Gate-1 reviewer disputed `overloaded`'s presence in the current enum — fixtures are generated from this snapshot and unknown values normalize to `unknown`, so drift is safe either way. Re-snapshot before freezing fixtures.
    15	- Limit strings (fallback tier): `You've hit your session limit · resets …` / `weekly limit` / `Opus limit`; throttle: `Server is temporarily limiting requests (not your usage limit)`; `API Error: Request rejected (429)`.
    16	- **Live capture (2026-07-12, this machine)**: a subagent died with `Agent terminated early due to an API error: You've hit your session limit · resets 2am (Asia/Dubai)` — reset hints can carry a timezone suffix in parentheses; the resetHint extractor and fixtures must cover this variant.
    17	- Attribution: `"attribution": {"commit": "", "pr": ""}` in settings.json (`includeCoAuthoredBy` is deprecated).
    18	
    19	## Codex CLI (>= 0.144; this machine: 0.144.1)
    20	
    21	- Lifecycle hooks: `SessionStart`, `SubagentStart`, `PreToolUse`, `PermissionRequest`, `PostToolUse`, `PreCompact`, `PostCompact`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `SubagentStop`, `Stop`. Config: `~/.codex/hooks.json`, `<repo>/.codex/hooks.json`, or config.toml `[hooks]` tables. Payload: stdin JSON with `session_id`, `cwd`, `hook_event_name`, `model`, `transcript_path` (+ `turn_id` for turn-scoped).
    22	- **Trust gate**: non-managed command hooks require review of the exact definition; trust recorded against the hook's hash; new/changed hooks skipped until re-trusted via `/hooks`. Project hooks load only when the project `.codex/` layer is trusted.
    23	- `commandWindows` / `command_windows` = Windows-only command override.
    24	- Custom prompts (`~/.codex/prompts/*.md`) are deprecated upstream — legacy fallback only.
    25	- Skills: Agent Skills standard at `.agents/skills` (repo) and `~/.agents/skills` (user).
    26	- Limit string: `You've hit your usage limit. … try again at [date/time]`; `codex exec --json` exits non-zero on failure (nonzero alone never classifies as usage-limit).
    27	- gpt-5.6-sol is server-gated by CLI version (rejected on 0.135.0 with "requires a newer version of Codex"; works on 0.144.1).
    28	
    29	## Cursor
    30	
    31	- Hook events (verified; cursor.com/docs/agent/hooks): `sessionStart`, `sessionEnd`, `preToolUse`, `postToolUse`, `postToolUseFailure`, `subagentStart`, `subagentStop`, `beforeShellExecution`, `afterShellExecution`, `beforeMCPExecution`, `afterMCPExecution`, `beforeReadFile`, `afterFileEdit`, `beforeTabFileRead`, `afterTabFileEdit`, `beforeSubmitPrompt`, `preCompact`, `afterAgentResponse`, `afterAgentThought`, `stop`, `workspaceOpen`.
    32	- **There is no pre-commit/VCS hook.** Agent-issued commits are observable via `beforeShellExecution` with a git-commit matcher; SCM-UI commits are invisible.
    33	- Config: `.cursor/hooks.json` (project; requires trusted workspace), `~/.cursor/hooks.json` (user), enterprise/MDM paths. Format: `{"version": 1, "hooks": {"<event>": [{"command", "timeout", "matcher"}]}}`.
    34	- No `commandWindows` variant — cross-platform command strings.
    35	- BYO-key rate-limit JSON: `{"error": "Too Many Requests", "message": "Rate limit exceeded…"}`; managed-billing error schema undocumented (heuristics, `confidence: low`).
    36	- cursor-agent reads `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` natively; `.cursor/commands` CLI loading unconfirmed — `baton receive --print-prompt` is the universal fallback.

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * Ensure an ignore pattern is active in a .gitignore body. Pure text
     3	 * transform: a covering non-comment line ('.handoff/' or '.handoff') makes
     4	 * this a byte-for-byte no-op; otherwise the pattern is appended on its own
     5	 * line with a single trailing newline.
     6	 * @param {string | null} existingText current .gitignore body, or null when absent
     7	 * @param {string} pattern the ignore entry to guarantee (e.g. '.handoff/')
     8	 * @returns {{text: string, changed: boolean}}
     9	 */
    10	export function ensureIgnoreLine(existingText, pattern) {
    11	  const body = existingText ?? '';
    12	  const bare = pattern.endsWith('/') ? pattern.slice(0, -1) : pattern;
    13	  const covers = body
    14	    .split(/\r?\n/)
    15	    .map((l) => l.trim())
    16	    .some((l) => l === pattern || l === bare);
    17	  if (covers) return { text: body, changed: false };
    18	
    19	  if (body === '') return { text: pattern + '\n', changed: true };
    20	  const joined = body.endsWith('\n') ? body : body + '\n';
    21	  return { text: joined + pattern + '\n', changed: true };
    22	}

/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba core/src/commands/init.mjs | sed -n '1,340p'" in /Users/saksham/baton
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	import { dirname } from 'node:path';
     2	import { planInit } from '../scaffold/plan.mjs';
     3	import { planHarnessInit } from '../scaffold/harness.mjs';
     4	import { atomicWriteText, ensureDir } from '../util/fsx.mjs';
     5	import { emitEnvelope, resolveRoot, parseFlagsStrict, usageError } from './shared.mjs';
     6	
     7	/**
     8	 * `baton init` — two-phase scaffolder. The plan phase (planInit) is read-only;
     9	 * `--dry-run` stops there. Apply writes each planned action atomically;
    10	 * refusals (malformed settings, corrupt markers) warn and leave their file
    11	 * untouched while the rest of the scaffold still lands.
    12	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    13	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    14	 */
    15	export async function cmdInit(args, io) {
    16	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { codex: 'boolean', cursor: 'boolean', 'with-legacy-prompts': 'boolean', force: 'boolean', check: 'boolean', 'dry-run': 'boolean' });
    17	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'init', parsed.error);
    18	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    19	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    20	
    21	  // The strict parser rejects stray tokens outright (a pasted `# comment` used
    22	  // to be consumed as --dry-run's "value", silently running a REAL init), so
    23	  // boolean flags here are only ever true | undefined.
    24	  const checkMode = flags.check === true;
    25	  const dryRun = flags['dry-run'] === true;
    26	
    27	  /** @type {ReturnType<typeof planInit>} */
    28	  let actions;
    29	  try {
    30	    const base = planInit(root, { force: flags.force === true }, io);
    31	    // Feed the harness writer the gitignore text AS THE BASE SCAFFOLD LEAVES IT
    32	    // (planned write, else current file) so its own ignore lines chain on top —
    33	    // two independent reads would make the later write clobber the earlier one.
    34	    const gi = base.find((a) => a.id === 'gitignore');
    35	    /** @type {string | null} */
    36	    let gitignoreBase = null;
    37	    if (gi?.op === 'write') {
    38	      gitignoreBase = /** @type {string} */ (gi.preview);
    39	    } else {
    40	      try {
    41	        gitignoreBase = io.fs.readFileSync(`${root}/.gitignore`, 'utf8');
    42	      } catch {
    43	        gitignoreBase = null;
    44	      }
    45	    }
    46	    actions = [
    47	      ...base,
    48	      ...planHarnessInit(
    49	        root,
    50	        { codex: flags.codex === true, cursor: flags.cursor === true, withLegacyPrompts: flags['with-legacy-prompts'] === true, gitignoreBase },
    51	        io,
    52	      ),
    53	    ];
    54	  } catch (err) {
    55	    const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err);
    56	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'plan-failed', msg } });
    57	    else io.stderr.write(`baton init: ${msg}\n`);
    58	    return 1;
    59	  }
    60	
    61	  const describe = (/** @type {any} */ a) =>
    62	    a.op === 'write' ? `write ${a.path}` : a.op === 'skip' ? `skip  ${a.path} (${a.note})` : `REFUSE ${a.path} — ${a.note}`;
    63	
    64	  // --check (gate-2 fix 9): the CI drift gate. Read-only — a scaffolded tree
    65	  // where init would change nothing passes; anything init would write or
    66	  // refuse is drift and fails the check.
    67	  if (checkMode) {
    68	    const drift = actions.filter((a) => a.op !== 'skip');
    69	    if (flags.json) {
    70	      emitEnvelope(io, {
    71	        ok: drift.length === 0,
    72	        data: { drift: drift.map(({ id, path, op, note }) => ({ id, path, op, note: note ?? null })) },
    73	      });
    74	    } else if (drift.length === 0) {
    75	      io.stdout.write('baton init --check: no drift — rendered files match template output\n');
    76	    } else {
    77	      io.stderr.write(`baton init --check: drift detected — init would write:\n${drift.map((a) => `  ${describe(a)}`).join('\n')}\n`);
    78	    }
    79	    return drift.length === 0 ? 0 : 1;
    80	  }
    81	
    82	  if (dryRun) {
    83	    if (flags.json) {
    84	      emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { dryRun: true, actions: actions.map(({ id, path, op, note }) => ({ id, path, op, note: note ?? null })) } });
    85	    } else {
    86	      io.stdout.write(`baton init plan (dry run — nothing written):\n${actions.map((a) => `  ${describe(a)}`).join('\n')}\n`);
    87	    }
    88	    return 0;
    89	  }
    90	
    91	  /** @type {string[]} */
    92	  const warnings = [];
    93	  for (const a of actions) {
    94	    if (a.op === 'refuse') {
    95	      warnings.push(a.note ?? `refused ${a.path}`);
    96	      continue;
    97	    }
    98	    if (a.op !== 'write') continue;
    99	    ensureDir(io.fs, dirname(a.path));
   100	    atomicWriteText(io.fs, a.path, /** @type {string} */ (a.preview));
   101	  }
   102	  for (const w of warnings) io.stderr.write(`baton init: ${w} — left untouched\n`);
   103	
   104	  if (flags.json) {
   105	    emitEnvelope(io, {
   106	      ok: true,
   107	      data: { actions: actions.map(({ id, path, op, note }) => ({ id, path, op, note: note ?? null })) },
   108	      warnings,
   109	    });
   110	  } else {
   111	    io.stdout.write(`baton init:\n${actions.map((a) => `  ${describe(a)}`).join('\n')}\n`);
   112	  }
   113	  return 0;
   114	}

    45	
    46	// Mirrors tests/unit/receive-prompt.test.mjs (and render.test.mjs) VERBATIM.
    47	// Deliberately narrow so it never trips on legitimate platform/model identifiers
    48	// ("claude-code", "gpt-5.6-sol") that are real data, not attribution.
    49	const ATTRIBUTION_PATTERNS = [
    50	  /co-authored-by/i,
    51	  /generated with claude/i,
    52	  /generated by claude/i,
    53	  /🤖/,
    54	  /written by (an? )?ai\b/i,
    55	  /authored by (an? )?ai\b/i,
    56	  /powered by claude/i,
    57	];
    58	const scanAttribution = (text) => ATTRIBUTION_PATTERNS.filter((re) => re.test(text)).map((re) => re.source);

    65	/** Recursively collect every file path under `dir` on the real fs. */
    66	function walkFiles(dir) {
    67	  /** @type {string[]} */
    68	  const out = [];
    69	  if (!existsSync(dir)) return out;
    70	  for (const name of readdirSync(dir)) {
    71	    const p = join(dir, name);
    72	    if (statSync(p).isDirectory()) out.push(...walkFiles(p));
    73	    else out.push(p);
    74	  }
    75	  return out;
    76	}

    88	    task: { goal: 'Scan every rendered output', constraints: ['Deterministic output'], acceptance: [] },
    89	    plan: { steps: [{ id: 's1', title: 'Render', status: 'active', note: null }] },
    90	    decisions: [{ seq: 1, ts: T0, summary: 'no attribution anywhere' }],
