     1	import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
     2	import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
     3	import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
     4	import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
     5	import { join, dirname } from 'node:path';
     6	import { makeIo } from '../helpers/fakeio.mjs';
     7	import { prepare, commit } from '../../core/src/receive/txn.mjs';
     8	import { loadBundle, bundlePaths, appendJournal } from '../../core/src/bundle/store.mjs';
     9	
    10	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    11	// Contract choices for core/src/receive/txn.mjs (docs/design/core.md §Module APIs
    12	// prepare/commit; §Per-module test lists "txn"; plan §Concurrency — the receive
    13	// prepare/commit two-phase transaction, receipt-token bindings incl. the
    14	// content-sensitive git digest, open->received degraded seal, ownership adoption +
    15	// new generation, chained failover, competing receivers, double-commit).
    16	//
    17	// SIGNATURES (pinned — the task overrides core.md's 3-arg commit):
    18	//   prepare(root, opts, io) -> { token, prompt, assignments, warnings }
    19	//   commit(root, token, opts, io) -> { adopted, generation, archivedTo }
    20	//   Both are SYNCHRONOUS. The plan pins that the receive COMMAND wires
    21	//   core/src/git/snapshot.mjs in and passes the result as opts.gitSnapshot; txn
    22	//   itself never spawns git — it takes the snapshot as plain data.
    23	//
    24	//   opts (same shape for prepare and commit):
    25	//     { platform,       // destination harness we are resuming ON (e.g. 'codex')
    26	//       origin,         // intake: the harness handed off FROM (e.g. 'claude-code')
    27	//       reason,         // intake: why the switch happened (free text)
    28	//       gitSnapshot,    // the caller-computed git snapshot object (git/snapshot.mjs)
    29	//       probes,         // optional probe map for resolveRoles (default null)
    30	//       sessionHint }   // the RECEIVING session's hint, used for ownership adoption
    31	//
    32	// TOKEN BINDING (THE pin). prepare captures a REVISION FINGERPRINT and returns it
    33	//   as an opaque token. It binds every one of these inputs (one rejection test per
    34	//   input below — verifier fold F3 completed the set):
    35	//     - bundle REVISION: generation + journalSeq (as loadBundle reports them)
    36	//     - intake: origin + reason
    37	//     - config digest: a hash of the baton.config.json bytes at <root>
    38	//     - GIT digest: a hash of the WHOLE opts.gitSnapshot object (PIN — the git
    39	//       state is bound by hashing the caller-passed object, so headSha, dirty,
    40	//       AND contentDigest are each binding: a HEAD move with an UNCHANGED
    41	//       contentDigest still drifts the token, and vice versa)
    42	//     - probe snapshot: a hash of opts.probes
    43	//     - target platform (opts.platform)
    44	//     - receiving session (opts.sessionHint) — plan: the token binds "target
    45	//       platform+session"
    46	//   prepare mutates NOTHING (it cannot persist a "prepared" record — the token is
    47	//   self-contained). commit RE-DERIVES every bound input from current disk state +
    48	//   its own opts and compares to the token; ANY drift throws an Error whose message
    49	//   names re-prepare (matches /re-?prepare/i) and performs NO mutation (the
    50	//   .handoff tree is byte-identical before and after the rejected commit). Because
    51	//   a successful commit bumps `generation`, an already-spent token (double-commit)
    52	//   and a losing competitor's token (competing receivers) both fail the revision
    53	//   check with no extra bookkeeping.
    54	//
    55	// COMMIT SUCCESS (one atomic transition):
    56	//   - Archives the current bundle to history/ via rotateJournal kind 'receive'
    57	//     (a history/<stem>.receive.json + .ndjson pair). archivedTo === that stem.
    58	//     The archived .receive freeze is the RECEIVED seal (handoff.status
    59	//     'received' — asserted on BOTH the sealed and open paths, verifier fold F7:
    60	//     archiving the pre-commit sealed bundle unchanged would be wrong); it
    61	//     retains the receipt data (the receive_log entry for THIS receive rides in
    62	//     the archived freeze). For an OPEN source bundle it is the receive-generated
    63	//     DEGRADED seal (handoff.reason from intake, reasonClass classified from the
    64	//     reason text against the intake ORIGIN platform via the built-in signature
    65	//     table); for a SEALED source the finalize reason/reasonClass are retained.
    66	//   - Adopts ownership: origin becomes the RECEIVING side — origin.platform ===
    67	//     opts.platform, origin.sessionHint === opts.sessionHint.
    68	//   - Opens a fresh writable generation: generation + 1, handoff.status 'open'.
    69	//   - Appends a receive_log entry { origin, reason, at }; for the OPEN (degraded)
    70	//     path the entry additionally carries degradedSeal: true (PIN: that flag lives
    71	//     on the receive_log entry). The sealed path's entry has NO truthy degradedSeal.
    72	//   - Returns { adopted: <truthy>, generation: <new gen>, archivedTo: <stem> }.
    73	//
    74	// STALENESS (prepare warnings, plan §Receive): OPEN source bundle -> a warning
    75	//   notes the missing seal; age > 12h (now - finalizedAt), HEAD moved
    76	//   (bundle.git.headSha !== opts.gitSnapshot.headSha), and a dirty mismatch each
    77	//   add a warning. Asserted as substring matches, not byte-exact strings.
    78	//
    79	// All txn tests seed: the bundle, baton.config.json at <root>, AND the built-in
    80	// signature table at its module-resolved path (loadSignatures reads via io.fs), so
    81	// role resolution and reasonClass classification both work over memfs.
    82	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    83	
    84	const here = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
    85	const repoRoot = join(here, '..', '..');
    86	// The exact path txn resolves for the built-in table (core/src/receive ->
    87	// ../../data), so seeding it here makes loadSignatures(io) succeed over memfs.
    88	const SIG_PATH = join(repoRoot, 'core', 'data', 'signatures.v1.json');
    89	const SIG_CONTENT = readFileSync(SIG_PATH, 'utf8');
    90	
    91	const ROOT = '/repo';
    92	const NOW = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z';
    93

   106	  defaults: { 'claude-code': 'claude-fable-5', codex: 'gpt-5.6-sol', cursor: 'composer' },
   107	};
   108	const CONFIG_TEXT = JSON.stringify(CONFIG, null, 2);
   109	
   110	// A git snapshot as core/src/git/snapshot.mjs would return; headSha matches the

   112	const G1 = { branch: 'main', headSha: 'abc123', dirty: false, dirtySummary: [], contentDigest: 'gd-1' };
   113	const G2 = { branch: 'main', headSha: 'abc123', dirty: false, dirtySummary: [], contentDigest: 'gd-2-CHANGED' };
   114	
   115	const snapText = (b) => JSON.stringify(b, null, 2) + '\n';
   116	
   117	function baseBundle(overrides = {}) {
   118	  return {
   119	    schema: 'baton/bundle@1',
   120	    bundleId: 'b_txn0000000000',
   121	    generation: 1,
   122	    createdAt: NOW,
   123	    updatedAt: NOW,
   124	    origin: { platform: 'claude-code', model: 'claude-fable-5', sessionHint: 'sess-A', unstable: false },
   125	    task: { goal: 'Wire the receive transaction', constraints: [], acceptance: [] },
   126	    plan: { steps: [{ id: 's1', title: 'Bind the token', status: 'active', note: null }] },
   127	    decisions: [],
   128	    files: { touched: [] },
   129	    roles: { assignments: {} },
   130	    git: { branch: 'main', headSha: 'abc123', dirty: false, dirtySummary: [], contentDigest: 'd0', summaryTruncated: false },
   131	    handoff: { status: 'open', reason: null, reasonClass: null, toPlatformHint: null, finalizedAt: null, receive_log: [] },
   132	    journalSeq: 5,
   133	    compaction: { droppedDecisions: 0, note: null },
   134	    dedupeRing: [],
   135	    ...overrides,
   136	  };
   137	}
   138	
   139	function sealedBundle(overrides = {}) {
   140	  return baseBundle({
   141	    handoff: { status: 'sealed', reason: "You've hit your usage limit", reasonClass: 'usage-limit', toPlatformHint: 'codex', finalizedAt: NOW, receive_log: [] },
   142	    ...overrides,
   143	  });
   144	}
   145	
   146	function openBundle(overrides = {}) {
   147	  return baseBundle({ handoff: { status: 'open', reason: null, reasonClass: null, toPlatformHint: null, finalizedAt: null, receive_log: [] }, ...overrides });
   148	}
   149	
   150	function makeTxnIo({ bundle, journal, configText = CONFIG_TEXT, now = NOW, extra = {} } = {}) {
   151	  const files = {
   152	    [SIG_PATH]: SIG_CONTENT,
   153	    [`${ROOT}/baton.config.json`]: configText,
   154	    [`${ROOT}/.handoff/bundle.json`]: snapText(bundle),
   155	    ...extra,
   156	  };
   157	  if (journal !== undefined) files[`${ROOT}/.handoff/journal.ndjson`] = journal;
   158	  return makeIo({ files, now });
   159	}
   160	
   161	// Standard opts for a claude-code -> codex receive.
   162	function opts(overrides = {}) {
   163	  return { platform: 'codex', origin: 'claude-code', reason: 'switching to codex', gitSnapshot: G1, probes: null, sessionHint: 'codex-sess-1', ...overrides };
   164	}

   179	    assert.ok(out.prompt.includes('unverified claims to check against the working tree'), 'prompt carries the claims posture');
   180	    assert.ok(out.assignments && typeof out.assignments === 'object', 'assignments is an object');
   181	    assert.deepEqual(Object.keys(out.assignments), Object.keys(CONFIG.roles), 'one assignment per configured role');
   182	    assert.ok(Array.isArray(out.warnings), 'warnings is an array');

   224	describe('receive-txn.commit — sealed -> received (normal), ownership adoption + new generation', () => {

   243	    assert.equal(bundle.origin.platform, 'codex', 'ownership adopted to the receiving platform');

   246	    assert.ok(entry, 'a receive_log entry was appended');

   272	describe('receive-txn.commit — open -> received (degraded seal)', () => {
   273	  it('writes a degraded seal: receive_log entry {degradedSeal:true}, reasonClass classified from the reason', () => {
   274	    // A usage-limit reason string for the claude-code origin -> classifies usage-limit.
   275	    const reason = "You've hit your session limit · resets 3pm";

   296	
   297	  it('(F10) a LOW-confidence classification is NOT sealed — reasonClass stays null (no laundering)', () => {
   298	    // A cursor "quota exceeded" reason matches usage-limit only at LOW
   299	    // confidence. Sealing it would make the NEXT prepare trust it as high
   300	    // confidence and auto-avoid the origin. The degraded seal must leave
   301	    // reasonClass null so the next receive re-classifies at its true confidence.
   302	    const io = makeTxnIo({ bundle: openBundle(), journal: '' });
   303	    const o = opts({ origin: 'cursor', reason: 'quota exceeded' });
   304	    const { token } = prepare(ROOT, o, io);
   305	    const res = commit(ROOT, token, o, io);
   306	
   307	    const seal = readHistoryFreeze(io, res.archivedTo);
   308	    assert.equal(seal.handoff.reasonClass, null, 'a low-confidence class is not laundered into the seal');
   309	
   310	    // And the fresh open generation likewise carries no trusted class.
   311	    const { bundle } = loadBundle(ROOT, io);
   312	    assert.equal(bundle.handoff.reasonClass, null, 'the new open generation inherits no low-confidence class');
   313	  });
   314	
   315	  it('(F10) an EXPLICIT --reason-class still seals (confirmed intake is high-confidence)', () => {
   316	    const io = makeTxnIo({ bundle: openBundle(), journal: '' });
   317	    const o = opts({ origin: 'cursor', reason: 'quota exceeded', reasonClass: 'usage-limit' });
   318	    const { token } = prepare(ROOT, o, io);
   319	    const res = commit(ROOT, token, o, io);
   320	    assert.equal(readHistoryFreeze(io, res.archivedTo).handoff.reasonClass, 'usage-limit', 'explicit intake seals');
   321	  });
   322	
   323	  it('(iter-4 I3) the fresh open generation carries NO prior reason/class/finalizedAt/toPlatformHint', () => {
   324	    // Even a HIGH-confidence sealed usage-limit receive must not leave its class
   325	    // on the new writable generation: the next prepare treats a sealed
   326	    // reasonClass as high-confidence and would auto-avoid the freshly adopted
   327	    // origin. Only receive_log (the audit chain) carries forward.
   328	    const io = makeTxnIo({ bundle: sealedBundle(), journal: '' });
   329	    const o = opts();
   330	    const { token } = prepare(ROOT, o, io);
   331	    const res = commit(ROOT, token, o, io);
   332	    assert.equal(res.generation, 2);
   333	
   334	    const { bundle } = loadBundle(ROOT, io);
   335	    assert.equal(bundle.handoff.status, 'open', 'a fresh writable generation');
   336	    assert.equal(bundle.handoff.reason, null, 'no carried reason');
   337	    assert.equal(bundle.handoff.reasonClass, null, 'no carried class (would launder as high-confidence next hop)');
   338	    assert.equal(bundle.handoff.finalizedAt, null, 'no carried finalizedAt (would poison the staleness reference)');
   339	    assert.equal(bundle.handoff.toPlatformHint, null, 'no carried toPlatformHint');
   340	    assert.ok(Array.isArray(bundle.handoff.receive_log) && bundle.handoff.receive_log.length >= 1, 'the receive_log audit chain is kept');
   341	  });
   342	
   343	  it('(iter-4 I3) a receive off the adopted generation does NOT auto-avoid the healthy origin', () => {
   344	    // End-to-end of the laundering fix: after a sealed usage-limit receive to
   345	    // claude-code, a NEXT receive (open generation, no seal) must re-classify at
   346	    // true confidence, not trust a carried class → claude-code stays selectable.
   347	    const io = makeTxnIo({ bundle: sealedBundle(), journal: '' });
   348	    const o1 = opts(); // claude-code -> codex per the helper default
   349	    commit(ROOT, prepare(ROOT, o1, io).token, o1, io);
   350	    // Now generation 2 is open, owned by codex. Receive it onward with a benign
   351	    // (non-limit) reason; the carried class must not drive avoidance.
   352	    const o2 = opts({ platform: 'claude-code', origin: 'codex', reason: 'switching back', sessionHint: 'cc-2' });
   353	    const { assignments } = prepare(ROOT, o2, io);
   354	    const avoidedCodex = Object.values(assignments).every((/** @type {any} */ a) =>
   355	      (a.skipped ?? []).some((/** @type {any} */ s) => s.platform === 'codex' && s.why === 'avoided'),
   356	    );
   357	    assert.equal(avoidedCodex, false, 'no carried class silently avoids the adopted origin');
   358	  });
   359	});

     1	import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
     2	import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
     3	import { resolveRoles } from '../../core/src/roles/resolve.mjs';
     4	
     5	// ===========================================================================
     6	// Contract choices for roles/resolve.mjs. Source of truth: docs/design/core.md
     7	// §Module APIs (resolveRoles signature + assignment shape); plan §Core engine —
     8	// Role matrix + Availability probing (capability/outcome dimensions, avoid[],
     9	// native/delegated/forced-default/unavailable, chainIndex + skipped audit data).
    10	// resolveRoles is PURE and explainable.
    11	//
    12	// INPUT: resolveRoles({config, to, avoid = [], nativeOnly = false, probes = null}).
    13	//   `config` is the PARSED config (roles as arrays of {platform, model, effort},
    14	//   as loadConfig returns) — built inline here so this file's red phase is
    15	//   attributable to resolve.mjs alone (not matrix.mjs).
    16	//
    17	// RETURN: { assignments: {[role]: Assignment}, notes: [] }.
    18	//   Assignment = {platform, model, effort, mode, chainIndex, skipped[], degraded?}.
    19	//   modes: 'native' | 'delegated' | 'forced-default' | 'unavailable'.
    20	//
    21	// PINS:
    22	//   1. CHAIN WALK. For each role, walk entries in order; the FIRST eligible
    23	//      (surviving) entry is selected. skipped[] records each passed-over entry
    24	//      as {chainIndex, platform, model, why} (extra fields tolerated).
    25	//      why values: 'avoided' | 'unknown-platform' | 'disabled' | 'rate-limited' | 'unauthenticated'.
    26	//   2. ELIGIBILITY / skip reasons:
    27	//        - platform ∈ avoid                          -> skip, why 'avoided'
    28	//        - platform NOT a key of config.platforms    -> skip, why 'unknown-platform'
    29	//        - config.platforms[p].enabled === false     -> skip, why 'disabled'
    30	//        - probes given & probes[p].outcome==='rate-limited' -> skip, why 'rate-limited'
    31	//        - probes given & capability==='installed' AND outcome!=='ok' (a VERIFIED
    32	//          auth/reachability FAILURE)                 -> skip, why 'unauthenticated'
    33	//          (iter-3 F1: a capped SUCCESS {installed, ok} leaves auth UNVERIFIED —
    34	//          it stays selectable and is flagged degraded, it is NOT skipped)
    35	//      A platform with capability ∈ {authenticated, reachable} AND outcome
    36	//      ≠ 'rate-limited' is eligible.
    37	//   3. DEGRADED. If probes is a non-null object but has NO entry for the chosen
    38	//      platform (unverifiable), the entry is selectable with degraded === true.
    39	//      probes === null means OFFLINE resolution: still selectable, but every
    40	//      selection is flagged degraded (gate-2 iter-2 M3; plan: offline always degraded).
    41	//      Non-degraded assignments have a falsy `degraded` (absent or false).
    42	//   4. MODE. First surviving entry: mode 'native' iff entry.platform === to,
    43	//      else 'delegated'. chainIndex = that entry's index in the ORIGINAL chain.
    44	//   5. nativeOnly restricts consideration to entries with platform === to; if
    45	//      none survive it MAY fall back to config.defaults[to] with mode
    46	//      'forced-default' (platform=to, model=defaults[to], effort=null,
    47	//      chainIndex=null) — but ONLY when the destination `to` is itself
    48	//      probe-eligible (PIN 2). When `to` is probe-INELIGIBLE (e.g. rate-limited),
    49	//      the forced-default is SUPPRESSED and the role resolves 'unavailable', the
    50	//      ineligibility audited in skipped[] (why 'rate-limited'). (fold C1-1)
    51	//   6. UNAVAILABLE. In normal (non-nativeOnly) mode, when NOTHING survives:
    52	//      mode 'unavailable', platform null, model null, effort null,
    53	//      chainIndex null, and skipped[] carries the full passed-over list.
    54	//   7. DETERMINISM. Same input -> deep-equal output; input config is not mutated.
    55	//      assignments object keys are in the CONFIG'S ROLE ORDER.
    56	//   8. avoid[] auto-population is the CALLER'S job; resolve only honors the list.
    57	// ===========================================================================
    58	
    59	const e = (platform, model, effort = null) => ({ platform, model, effort });
    60	
    61	const PLATFORMS = { 'claude-code': {}, codex: {}, cursor: {} };
    62	const DEFAULTS = { 'claude-code': 'claude-fable-5', codex: 'gpt-5.6-sol', cursor: 'composer' };
    63	
    64	// The full seven-role config in parsed form (mirrors the real baton.config.json).
    65	function fullConfig() {
    66	  return {
    67	    schema: 'baton/config@1',
    68	    roles: {
    69	      planner: [e('claude-code', 'claude-fable-5')],
    70	      'plan-reviewer': [e('codex', 'gpt-5.6-sol', 'xhigh'), e('claude-code', 'claude-fable-5', 'xhigh')],
    71	      'test-author': [e('claude-code', 'claude-opus-4-8'), e('codex', 'gpt-5.5', 'xhigh')],
    72	      'test-verifier': [e('codex', 'gpt-5.5', 'xhigh'), e('claude-code', 'claude-opus-4-8')],
    73	      implementer: [e('claude-code', 'claude-fable-5'), e('codex', 'gpt-5.6-sol', 'xhigh'), e('cursor', 'composer')],
    74	      'final-reviewer-a': [e('codex', 'gpt-5.6-sol', 'xhigh'), e('codex', 'gpt-5.5', 'xhigh')],
    75	      'final-reviewer-b': [e('claude-code', 'claude-fable-5', 'xhigh'), e('claude-code', 'claude-opus-4-8')],
    76	    },
    77	    platforms: { ...PLATFORMS },
    78	    defaults: { ...DEFAULTS },
    79	  };
    80	}
    81	
    82	// A small single-role config helper.
    83	function oneRole(chain) {
    84	  return { schema: 'baton/config@1', roles: { r: chain }, platforms: { ...PLATFORMS }, defaults: { ...DEFAULTS } };
    85	}
    86	
    87	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

   135	  });
   136	
   137	  function pick(a) {
   138	    return { platform: a.platform, model: a.model, effort: a.effort, mode: a.mode, chainIndex: a.chainIndex };
   139	  }
   140	});
   141	
   142	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   143	describe('resolveRoles — mode selection', () => {
   144	  it('native: first surviving entry whose platform === to', () => {
   145	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({ config: oneRole([e('codex', 'gpt-5.5', 'xhigh')]), to: 'codex' });

   150

   161

   168	    assert.equal(assignments.r.mode, 'unavailable');
   169	    assert.equal(assignments.r.platform, null);
   170	    assert.equal(assignments.r.model, null);
   171	    assert.equal(assignments.r.effort, null);
   172	    assert.equal(assignments.r.chainIndex, null);
   173	    assert.equal(assignments.r.skipped.length, 2);
   174	  });
   175	});
   176	
   177	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   178	describe('resolveRoles — nativeOnly + forced-default', () => {
   179	  it('nativeOnly picks a native entry, skipping delegated ones', () => {
   180	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({
   181	      config: oneRole([e('claude-code', 'claude-fable-5'), e('codex', 'gpt-5.6-sol', 'xhigh')]),
   182	      to: 'codex',
   183	      nativeOnly: true,
   184	    });
   185	    assert.equal(assignments.r.mode, 'native');
   186	    assert.equal(assignments.r.platform, 'codex');
   187	    assert.equal(assignments.r.chainIndex, 1);
   188	  });
   189	
   190	  it('nativeOnly with no native entry falls back to defaults[to] as forced-default (no probing)', () => {
   191	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({
   192	      config: oneRole([e('claude-code', 'claude-fable-5')]),
   193	      to: 'codex',
   194	      nativeOnly: true,
   195	    });
   196	    assert.equal(assignments.r.mode, 'forced-default');
   197	    assert.equal(assignments.r.platform, 'codex');
   198	    assert.equal(assignments.r.model, 'gpt-5.6-sol'); // defaults.codex
   199	    assert.equal(assignments.r.effort, null);
   200	    assert.equal(assignments.r.chainIndex, null);
   201	  });
   202	
   203	  it('nativeOnly forced-default requires the destination `to` to be probe-ELIGIBLE (authenticated/ok)', () => {
   204	    // No native codex entry in the chain, but codex (the destination) is probed
   205	    // eligible -> the forced-default IS selectable. This ties the forced-default
   206	    // fallback to destination probe-eligibility (fold C1-1).
   207	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({
   208	      config: oneRole([e('claude-code', 'claude-fable-5')]),
   209	      to: 'codex',
   210	      nativeOnly: true,
   211	      probes: { codex: { capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'ok' } },
   212	    });
   213	    assert.equal(assignments.r.mode, 'forced-default');
   214	    assert.equal(assignments.r.platform, 'codex');
   215	    assert.equal(assignments.r.model, 'gpt-5.6-sol');
   216	    assert.equal(assignments.r.effort, null);
   217	    assert.equal(assignments.r.chainIndex, null);
   218	  });
   219	
   220	  it('nativeOnly: the only native entry (=to) is rate-limited -> forced-default SUPPRESSED, unavailable with a rate-limited skip', () => {
   221	    // The destination platform itself is rate-limited, so forcing its default is
   222	    // not valid: the role resolves 'unavailable', and the passed-over rate-limited
   223	    // entry is audited in skipped[] with why 'rate-limited' (fold C1-1, replaces
   224	    // the earlier test that wrongly selected a rate-limited forced-default).
   225	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({
   226	      config: oneRole([e('codex', 'gpt-5.6-sol', 'xhigh')]),
   227	      to: 'codex',
   228	      nativeOnly: true,
   229	      probes: { codex: { capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'rate-limited' } },
   230	    });
   231	    assert.equal(assignments.r.mode, 'unavailable');
   232	    assert.equal(assignments.r.platform, null);
   233	    assert.equal(assignments.r.model, null);
   234	    assert.equal(assignments.r.effort, null);
   235	    assert.equal(assignments.r.chainIndex, null);
   236	    const rl = assignments.r.skipped.find((s) => s.why === 'rate-limited' && s.platform === 'codex');
   237	    assert.ok(rl, 'the rate-limited destination entry must be audited in skipped[] with why "rate-limited"');
   238	  });
   239	});
   240	
   241	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   242	describe('resolveRoles — skip reasons', () => {
   243	  it("unknown platform (absent from config.platforms) -> skip, why 'unknown-platform'", () => {
   244	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({
   245	      config: oneRole([e('ghost', 'x'), e('codex', 'gpt-5.5', 'xhigh')]),
   246	      to: 'codex',
   247	    });
   248	    assert.equal(assignments.r.platform, 'codex');
   249	    assert.equal(assignments.r.chainIndex, 1);
   250	    assert.equal(assignments.r.skipped[0].why, 'unknown-platform');
   251	    assert.equal(assignments.r.skipped[0].platform, 'ghost');
   252	  });
   253	
   254	  it("probe outcome 'rate-limited' -> skip, why 'rate-limited'", () => {
   255	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({
   256	      config: oneRole([e('codex', 'a'), e('cursor', 'b')]),
   257	      to: 'cursor',
   258	      probes: { codex: { capability: 'reachable', outcome: 'rate-limited' }, cursor: { capability: 'reachable', outcome: 'ok' } },
   259	    });
   260	    assert.equal(assignments.r.platform, 'cursor');
   261	    assert.equal(assignments.r.chainIndex, 1);
   262	    assert.equal(assignments.r.skipped[0].why, 'rate-limited');
   263	    assert.equal(assignments.r.skipped[0].platform, 'codex');
   264	  });
   265	
   266	  it("capability 'installed' with a capped SUCCESS -> selectable + degraded, not skipped (iter-3 F1)", () => {
   267	    // A --version success caps capability at 'installed' but leaves auth
   268	    // UNVERIFIED (not verified-failed), so the platform stays selectable and is
   269	    // flagged degraded — per plan §Role matrix. Only a verified failure skips.
   270	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({
   271	      config: oneRole([e('codex', 'a'), e('cursor', 'b')]),
   272	      to: 'cursor',
   273	      probes: { codex: { capability: 'installed', outcome: 'ok' }, cursor: { capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'ok' } },
   274	    });
   275	    assert.equal(assignments.r.platform, 'codex', 'auth-unverified codex stays selectable at chain head');
   276	    assert.equal(assignments.r.degraded, true, 'the auth-unverified selection is flagged degraded');
   277	    assert.equal(assignments.r.skipped.length, 0);
   278	  });
   279	
   280	  it("capability 'installed' with a VERIFIED failure -> skip, why 'unauthenticated' (iter-3 F1)", () => {

role: plan-reviewer
model: codex/gpt-5.5 @ xhigh
harness: Codex
date: 2026-07-18
degraded: model-fallback from unavailable gpt-5.6-sol

Verified facts:
The current CLI has only the v1 command set; `loop` and `pipeline` are new surfaces, while `wrap` is still reserved as future PTY supervisor work ([core/src/cli.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/cli.mjs:16), [core/src/cli.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/cli.mjs:97)). The existing role matrix has seven roles and no `merger` or `subtask-review` role ([baton.config.json](/Users/saksham/baton/baton.config.json:3)). Existing receive is a two-phase transaction whose token binds generation, journal seq, handoff status, intake, config digest, git digest, probe digest, target platform, and target session ([core/src/receive/txn.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/receive/txn.mjs:54), [core/src/receive/txn.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/receive/txn.mjs:80)); stale commits are rejected ([core/src/receive/txn.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/receive/txn.mjs:242)). Existing checkpointing enforces one active stable session per bundle and rejects foreign stable sessions unless `--take-over` is used ([core/src/commands/checkpoint.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/checkpoint.mjs:265)). Linked worktree commands will resolve their own worktree root because root discovery stops at nearest `.handoff`, `baton.config.json`, or `.git` ([core/src/commands/shared.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/shared.mjs:27)). Current `.gitignore` ignores `.handoff/` but not `.worktrees/` ([.gitignore](/Users/saksham/baton/.gitignore:1)).

Hypotheses:
The headless child contract will fail in practice unless the plan controls process groups, hook side effects, prompt echo in verdict parsing, live CLI permission modes, and smoke approval staleness. These are not implementation nits; they are the core reliability surface of this milestone.

VERDICT: BLOCKED
FINDINGS:
1. [blocking] — The plan violates the role-matrix invariant and introduces unresolved roles/hardcoded model identities. It says roles resolve through `baton.config.json` ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:41)), but the phase list uses `subtask-review` and the pipeline uses a `merger role (Fable)` ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:38), [docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:85)). Those roles are absent from the committed matrix ([baton.config.json](/Users/saksham/baton/baton.config.json:3)), and hardcoding “Sol / Claude / Fable” conflicts with the repo rule that concrete models live in config, not docs/prompts/code ([AGENTS.md](/Users/saksham/baton/AGENTS.md:16)). — Fix: Define exact role IDs for writer, reviewer, subtask reviewer, and merger in `baton.config.json`/template, or map them explicitly to existing roles. Remove concrete model/vendor names from the plan and child prompts; make loop spec validation fail when a referenced role is missing.

2. [blocking] — The central loop state does not compose with Layer-1 root discovery and one-active-session semantics. The plan wants one root `.handoff/loop` state plus two persistent worktrees ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:48), [docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:78)), but existing root discovery in a linked worktree will resolve the worktree root, not the main root ([core/src/commands/shared.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/shared.mjs:27)). Existing checkpoint rejects stable foreign sessions in the same bundle ([core/src/commands/checkpoint.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/checkpoint.mjs:275)), so simultaneous writer/reviewer child hooks can either fork state into per-worktree `.handoff/` dirs or be rejected/corrupt takeover flow. — Fix: Specify a single authoritative root passed to every child via `--root`, or explicitly namespace per-worker bundles. Define whether child hooks are disabled, redirected, or supervisor-owned. Add tests with two stable child session hints from two worktrees writing/checkpointing concurrently.

3. [blocking] — The failover chain is underspecified relative to the receive transaction. The plan says `detect → finalize → remap → receive → relaunch` ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:66)), but `baton remap` is read-only ([core/src/commands/remap.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/remap.mjs:6)), and `receive` requires prepare/commit with identical bound inputs and session/root/probe/git state ([core/src/commands/receive.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/receive.mjs:33), [core/src/commands/receive.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/receive.mjs:64)). Probe cache and git state are token inputs ([core/src/receive/txn.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/receive/txn.mjs:65)), so any supervisor write, probe refresh, ff-sync, or smoke artifact change between prepare and commit can invalidate the token. — Fix: Replace the chain with an exact transaction: freeze child output, classify, finalize or degraded-open receive, refresh probes before prepare, prepare+commit with identical flags/session/root and no intervening writes, then relaunch. Add stale-token and retry tests around loop-state writes, git changes, probe-cache changes, and open-bundle limit death.

4. [blocking] — “Role’s next chain entry” is not supported for model-level failures, including the degradation case in this review. The resolver skips by platform avoid/rate-limit/disabled/auth state, not by `{platform, model}` ([core/src/roles/resolve.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/roles/resolve.mjs:16)). It selects the first eligible entry in each configured role chain ([core/src/roles/resolve.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/roles/resolve.mjs:61)). If `codex/gpt-5.6-sol` is account-tier rejected but Codex itself is usable, the loop can retry the same rejected model forever or avoid all Codex entries and skip valid `codex/gpt-5.5` fallbacks. — Fix: Add model-entry failure state or an `avoidEntries[]` mechanism, classify model-unavailable separately from usage-limit, and test `gpt-5.6-sol rejected → codex/gpt-5.5 selected` without platform-wide avoidance.

5. [major] — The worktree discipline lacks corruption guards. The plan says two persistent worktrees are gitignored and branch-jailed, with only the merger writing main ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:78)), but current `.gitignore` does not ignore `.worktrees/` ([.gitignore](/Users/saksham/baton/.gitignore:1)). It also does not define stale worktree adoption, branch-name namespace, dirty-worktree refusal, merge-conflict parking, concurrent main movement, or `git worktree remove/prune` cleanup. HYPOTHESIS: raw deletion of `.worktrees/` can leave Git’s linked-worktree metadata stale. — Fix: Define `worktrees.mjs` as a strict Git transaction layer: namespaced branches, clean-index preflight, expected branch/HEAD assertions before and after each child, main merge lock, ff-only sync checks, conflict parking, and prune/remove cleanup. Add adversarial tests for stale branches, dirty workers, main moved, merge conflict, and untracked `.worktrees/`.

6. [major] — The attribution invariant is not actually covered for child worktree commits. The plan claims the existing doctor guard covers main and both worktree branches ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:89)), but current doctor scans only the last 50 commits reachable from the current repo invocation ([core/src/commands/doctor.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/doctor.mjs:229)). The hard repo invariant requires sole author and zero AI attribution everywhere ([AGENTS.md](/Users/saksham/baton/AGENTS.md:55)). — Fix: Make the supervisor set `GIT_AUTHOR_*` and `GIT_COMMITTER_*` for every child, disable Claude attribution settings where applicable, and gate every branch merge on `git log` scans over the exact worker branch and main range for author/email plus forbidden trailers. Acceptance must include child commits from both worktrees.

7. [major] — The supervisor spawn and verdict-tail contract is too weak for a v1 automation surface. The plan relies on plain final-tail parsing ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:52)), but does not specify process-group lifecycle, timeout kill/reap behavior, output truncation, prompt-echo defense, hook/log noise separation, or what happens when a child exits after edits but before a verdict. The existing detector classifies generic nonzero as `other-error`, not usage-limit ([core/src/detect/classifier.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/detect/classifier.mjs:81)). — Fix: Define child supervision as a contract: detached supervisor PID/lock, process groups, SIGTERM/SIGKILL escalation, transcript files with byte caps, parser over the child’s final assistant message only or structured output schema, and tests for prompt echo, multiple verdict tails, truncated transcript, timeout, zombie grandchild, and non-limit failure.

8. [major] — The smoke gate can approve stale state. The plan parks after smoke and resumes with `--approve-smoke` ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:61)), but it does not bind approval to smoke output, git state, loop state, or the exact command. Existing git snapshots include a content-sensitive digest that could be used for this ([core/src/git/snapshot.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/git/snapshot.mjs:17)). — Fix: Write a smoke approval record with digest of loop state, command, output, HEAD, dirty/contentDigest, and child assignment. Require `--approve-smoke <token>` and revalidate before continuing; otherwise park for a fresh smoke run.

The existing detector classifies generic nonzero as `other-error`, not usage-limit ([core/src/detect/classifier.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/detect/classifier.mjs:81)). — Fix: Define child supervision as a contract: detached supervisor PID/lock, process groups, SIGTERM/SIGKILL escalation, transcript files with byte caps, parser over the child’s final assistant message only or structured output schema, and tests for prompt echo, multiple verdict tails, truncated transcript, timeout, zombie grandchild, and non-limit failure.

8. [major] — The smoke gate can approve stale state. The plan parks after smoke and resumes with `--approve-smoke` ([docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md:61)), but it does not bind approval to smoke output, git state, loop state, or the exact command. Existing git snapshots include a content-sensitive digest that could be used for this ([core/src/git/snapshot.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/git/snapshot.mjs:17)). — Fix: Write a smoke approval record with digest of loop state, command, output, HEAD, dirty/contentDigest, and child assignment. Require `--approve-smoke <token>` and revalidate before continuing; otherwise park for a fresh smoke run.
